One select policy (`calendar_event_links_select`) checks the owner first, then `event_id in (select private.calendar_guest_events())`: the events whose organiser or attendees include one of the reader's proven addresses (`private.proven_addresses(user, workspace)`: the person's login email always counts; the address of a Microsoft mailbox or calendar account they connected counts only when its domain is the domain of a login email of a member of that workspace, because Graph's `mail` field is set by whichever tenant the person signs in to and a member's own tenant could otherwise claim a client's address; a disconnected account still counts, since its address was proven when it connected; the unverified other addresses from Settings never count). Everyone else sees only `online_meeting_url`. |
| `crm.hidden_attendees` | Addresses a user hid from People you've met, keyed by user, workspace, and email, visible only to that user. |

Join links (`lib/meeting-links.ts`, shared by Graph and calendar links): the provider's own link wins (`onlineMeeting.joinUrl`, then `onlineMeetingUrl`; for calendar links `X-GOOGLE-CONFERENCE`, then `X-MICROSOFT-SKYPETEAMSMEETINGURL`). Otherwise it is the first recognised meeting room link in the location, then the body. Those two go in `crm.calendar_events.online_meeting_url`, which teammates could already see (the location is shared). A link found only in the body goes in `crm.calendar_event_links` instead, and Calendar, the Meetings card, daily notes and push alerts show `coalesce(online_meeting_url, join_url)` as far as row-level security lets the reader see it. Recognised rooms: Teams (`/l/meetup-join/…`, `/meet/<id>`, a launcher or `/v2` link whose query or hash holds a meetup-join), Zoom and ZoomGov (`/j/`, `/my/`, `/w/`, `/wc/` with an id), Google Meet (`abc-defg-hij` and ten-letter codes, and `/lookup/`), Whereby (one path segment, including team subdomains), Webex (`j.php`, `/meet/<name>`, `/join/<name>`, the join service; not `help.` or `www.`), GoTo (`meet.goto.com/<room>`, `gotomeeting.com/join/<id>`, `gotomeet.me/<name>`), Jitsi (`meet.jit.si/<room>`, `8x8.vc`), Around, Chime (ten-digit ids, named rooms on `chime.aws` and `/meetings/`), and the user's own meeting link from Settings › General (exactly, or a path under it when it has a path; a query string must match). Help, install, download, about, terms, pricing, static, join, new, meetings, getstarted, newmeeting and similar first path segments, app roots and Teams meeting options never count, so a real room link anywhere in the text wins over them. Markdown links are cut at `](`. Outlook Safe Links (including `&amp;url=` and `Url=`), Google redirect links (Google's own domains only) and Proofpoint `urldefense` links (v2 and v3) are unwrapped, in the Join link and in the notes. Only `http` and `https` links are used, a recognised `http` link is stored as `https`, and links over 2,000 characters are skipped. The body is read as text and at most 200,000 characters of it are scanned, cut back to the last space or tag boundary so a link is never half read and a character never split; lone surrogates are dropped before anything is stored. HTML (an Orbit-created event's response, or a calendar link's description that has real tags such as `<br>`, `<p>`, a closing tag or an `<a href>`) goes through `htmlBodyText`, a single linear pass with `indexOf` (no backtracking patterns), which keeps each link's target in angle brackets after its label, drops `head`, `style`, `script` and Office `xml` contents, comments, conditional comments, `<!DOCTYPE>`, `<?xml?>` and namespaced Office tags such as `<o:p>`, collapses spaces as HTML does, and leaves text such as `<https://…>` or `x <p and y` as written. Bidirectional control characters are removed, from locations too when they are shown. Notes are capped at 8,000 characters because every event's notes travel with the Calendar page; the Join link is found in the whole scanned body first. A calendar link's description is converted once per event, not once per instance. Both are written on every sync, which re-reads the whole window, so existing Microsoft events gain them on their next sync (the calendar cron runs every ten minutes), and calendar links within six hours. Booking's live check asks Graph for events without bodies.

The event panel shows the notes under Description as plain text (never HTML), with links and email addresses linked (`components/_ui/linked-text.tsx`, built on `textParts` in `lib/text-links.ts`, which Mail's plain-text linkify also uses). Web links open in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. The location is linked the same way. Long notes are clamped to eight lines with Show more; while clamped, links below the fold get `tabindex="-1"` so keyboard focus skips them; screen readers still read the whole description. The assistant's `list_calendar_events` returns the notes of the user's own events in full, with `descriptionFromOutside` (a description on an event whose organiser is missing, unreadable or not one of the workspace members' proven addresses, from `private.workspace_member_addresses`; that lookup only runs when a listed event has a description) and `joinFromDescription`; `get_overview` carries only `hasDescription`, and leaves out a join link taken from an outside description.

Migration `20261022100000_multi_calendar.sql` creates a `__default__` calendar for each existing mailbox connection and moves its events onto it, so nothing is lost and no one reconnects. The first sync renames that row to the real default calendar's Graph id, keeping its settings and event rows.

Every sync upserts the events it read. After a complete read of a calendar it deletes that calendar's events that Graph no longer returned (removed, moved out of the window, or older than it), except events Orbit created (`created_by_orbit`) that start after the window, which the sync can't see yet; a partial read only drops events that ended before the window. Cancelled meetings are kept with `is_cancelled` and hidden everywhere. Links are recomputed for all of the user's events after each sync, after Create contact and after Use for CRM changes: events in calendars not used for CRM have no links, and in the others an organiser or attendee who is not internal (the workspace members' addresses, their other addresses, and their non-free-mail domains, plus the mailbox and calendar account addresses) links the active contact with that email and that contact's company, plus active companies with that domain unless it is a free-mail domain.

Missing permission: a connection made before `Calendars.Read` was added keeps working for mail. A token refresh never sends `scope`, so old connections keep refreshing. When the token response's `scope` lacks `Calendars.Read` (or `Calendars.ReadWrite`), or listing calendars answers 403 `ErrorAccessDenied`, the sync sets `scope_missing` and stops without an error; Capture (one line), Settings › Email and the Meetings card then ask to reconnect. Reconnecting clears the flag and runs a forced sync after the redirect. Write access is tracked the same way: the callback and every token refresh store the token response's `scope` in `integration_state.granted_scopes`, and `grantsCalendarWrite` reads it. No `Calendars.ReadWrite` there means `canWriteCalendar = false` and the invite dialog asks to reconnect; reads and mail are untouched. When nothing is stored yet (a connection from before this change whose access token is still valid), sending an invite first refreshes the token to learn the scopes. Orbit does not decode Graph access tokens, since Microsoft documents them as opaque to clients; it only reads `tid` from the ID token issued to Orbit itself.

Meetings card: up to five meetings that have not ended (the first shown as Next) and five that have, for the contact (`contact_ids`) or company (`company_ids`), from calendars used for CRM, with the same meeting in two calendars (same iCal uid) shown once. A Granola `meeting` activity within two hours of the start, on the same contact (or, on a company page, on the company or one of the meeting's contacts), is linked as Notes. People you've met: outside organisers and attendees of non-cancelled events in calendars used for CRM, without an active contact or a hidden entry, grouped by address with their last and next meeting, people met most recently first, then upcoming-only people, at most 25. Create contact checks the address is in the user's calendar and goes through `createContactFromAttendee`, the same path Unfiled conversations uses.

Calendar page (`app/(crm)/calendar`): `?date=YYYY-MM-DD&view=day|week`. Without `view` it shows the week on large screens and the day as an agenda list below that. The server loads the visible calendars' non-cancelled events overlapping the range (padded a day for all-day events) with contact ids for every attendee and the overlapping Granola note. Ranges and positions are London wall-clock days (`lib/calendar-view.ts`), so DST days are 23 or 25 hours; all-day events, which Graph returns at UTC midnight, sit on their calendar date. Overlapping events are packed into side-by-side columns per cluster. Toggling a calendar updates `is_visible` optimistically.

### Time blocking (Phases 2 to 6)

Task blocks (time blocking Phase 2): `readPlanner` (`lib/crm/task-blocks.ts`) reads, per workspace the viewer belongs to and through `withActor` as that workspace's actor, every open task they own or that is unassigned (the Tasks page's "mine" filter, with no cap, since the left menu lists them all) and their own timed tasks done today (`listPlannerTasks`), with each workspace's members, admins and tagged people for the Tasks panel. `taskBlocks` (`lib/task-blocks.ts`, client-safe) turns the viewer's own open timed tasks, and those done today, into blocks from `dueAt` and `durationMinutes`, flags a block overdue once it has ended (a date-only task after its day), names the first Busy meeting it overlaps (not free, tentative, declined or all-day), and names the workspace only for someone in more than one. Tasks they only created, are tagged in or see as an admin never become blocks; unassigned ones only show in the list. The grid packs blocks with meetings through `packColumns` (blocks at least 15 minutes tall, meetings 20) and the phone agenda sorts them among meetings. The left menu has two sections, Calendars (collapsed by default) and Tasks (grouped Overdue, Today, This week, Later and No date on London days by `groupTaskList`), remembered per browser under `orbit.calendar.sections`. A block or a list row opens the Tasks panel; the block's circle completes it through `useTaskDone` (held Done with Undo). Task server actions resolve the task's own workspace with `actorForTask` (`lib/crm/workspaces.ts`), so the panel saves, completes, links, searches links for and discusses a task from another workspace in that workspace, with that workspace's role. The lookup runs as the viewer under row-level security; a task they can't see stays on their current actor and fails as not found, and if the lookup itself fails the action refuses with "Couldn't find that task. Try again." rather than acting in the current workspace. The Done beacon resolves each id once and pushes to ClickUp as the same actor. Record links in the panel for a task from another workspace go through `/open` (`TaskRecordLink`, `components/crm/task-workspace.tsx`), which switches workspace and opens the record, and the panel hides the record's Docs notes for such a task. Closing the panel returns focus to the block or list row that opened it, else the grid; a task reassigned to someone else closes the panel, since it leaves the viewer's calendar. Tentative meetings never count as a clash, as the plan counts Busy meetings only. Cost: on a local database with 100,000 tasks in one workspace (20,000 open, 66,000 owned by the viewer), the planner read takes about 80 ms. The open branch uses `tasks_open_due_idx`, but the done-today branch reads every task the viewer owns through `tasks_owner_id_idx`. A follow-up migration can add `create index concurrently tasks_owner_completed_idx on crm.tasks (owner_id, completed_at) where archived_at is null` if production volumes call for it (they weren't measured for this change). The page's 60 second and focus refresh, and `router.refresh` after Done, Schedule and Duration, redraw the blocks.

Drag and resize (time blocking Phase 3): `useTaskDrag` (`hooks/use-task-drag.ts`) is the one drag hook for the Calendar page, built on `startPointerGesture` (`lib/pointer-gesture.ts`): a mouse picks up after 4px, a touch after a 350 ms hold (a ring fills at the finger, `animate-press-fill`), and a touch that moves first scrolls instead. `startPointerGesture` cancels a mouse or pen gesture when a context menu opens (a ctrl-click on a Mac) or a move arrives with no button held (a lost `pointerup`), and returns a cancel function, which the hook calls on unmount so no listener, animation frame or save outlives the page. One drag runs at a time, so a second finger during the hold is ignored. The hook picks up a task from the left menu's list, a block (moving it) or a block's bottom edge (resizing it), and does its own hit testing from the grid's `[data-calendar-scroller]` and `[data-day]` columns, so the grid only draws what the hook reports: the snapped slot with its day, time and duration, the lifted card at the pointer, the block ghosted where it was, the resized block, and the edge it is scrolling. A drag only counts once the pointer has moved 8px from where the drag started (6px for a resize), or, for a task from the list, has left where it started (the drawer, on a tablet); only then does the drawer close, the slot or drop zone show or the grid scroll, so a hold, or a click that wobbles, changes nothing. A mouse drag that never counted opens the task, as the click would have, and the click itself is always swallowed so it can't start a new event. Once it counts, each animation frame scrolls the grid when the pointer is within 48px of its top or bottom edge, faster nearer the edge (`edgeScrollSpeed`), never while the pointer is outside the grid, and toward the edge the drag started in only after the pointer has left that edge's zone or gone 16px deeper into it, so dragging an 08:00 block down or shrinking it doesn't scroll the grid away. The slot is worked out again on release, so a wheel scroll during the drag still drops in the right place. The maths is client-safe in `lib/task-drag.ts`: a move snaps its start to 15 minutes of wall time inside its London day, and the slot carries one instant (`moveInstant`) that both the label and the save use, which is the block's own `dueAt` when the wall slot hasn't changed, so a block at the repeated 01:30 when the clocks go back stays put, and a slot in the hour that doesn't exist when they go forward shows and saves the hour after; a resize keeps the task's own `dueAt` and measures the new length between instants (`resizedDuration`), in 15 minute steps from 15 minutes to 8 hours, never past the midnight after its start, and a block that already crosses midnight can shrink but not grow or collapse. The resize handle starts right of the done circle, only on the part of the block that holds its end, and a tap on it opens the task; on a coarse pointer it is a grip in the block's bottom-right corner (32px wide, up to 16px tall, with the text kept clear of it), never outside the block, so the rest of the block keeps hold-to-move and nothing reaches the block or meeting below. Dropping on the grid sends `{ at, durationMinutes }` and dropping a block on the left menu (`[data-task-drop]`, "Keep the day, remove the time"; a translucent bar over the grid's day headings, outside the scroller, labelled "Remove the time" in the time gutter so every heading stays readable, shown once the drag counts and only when the menu is hidden, as on an iPad in portrait; a drop zone only counts once the pointer has been outside it during the drag) sends `{ dateOnly: true }` to `placeTaskAction` (`app/(crm)/records.ts`), which resolves the task's own workspace with `actorForTask` and calls `placeTask` (`lib/crm/tasks.ts`). `placeTask` refuses a task outside the actor's workspace as not found, checks the person may change it, refuses a done task, and writes only what changed: the time with `scheduled_by` `person` (also when only the duration changed, through `applyTask`'s `claimSchedule`, so a resized planner block becomes the person's), the duration, the owner for an unassigned task, or the task date only on its own London day (clearing `scheduled_by` and the reminder). A drop that changes nothing records nothing and pushes nothing; otherwise it records `task.updated` with provenance `drag_task` from `web` and pushes to ClickUp after the response. Reminders are an offset from `due_at`, so they move with the block, and the browser's reminder poll keys on `dueAt` (there is no push marker to reset yet). The screen applies the drop at once (`placedTask`), keeps it until the refreshed planner shows it (`placementLanded`), and on failure puts the block back and shows the error, "Couldn't move the task. Try again." for anything unexpected; a failure overtaken by a later drop of the same task is ignored. After a drop the next click is swallowed unless a new press comes first, so a drag never also opens the task or starts a new event. Drag is on from the `md` breakpoint, where the grid shows; `startPointerGesture`'s `touchTarget` option keeps a touch followed after the drawer unmounts its row, since touch events stay with the element the finger first touched. Phones use Schedule. Clashes are allowed and flagged as before.

Working hours (time blocking Phase 4): `crm.working_hours` holds one row per person and weekday (`weekday` 0 for Sunday to 6, `works`, `starts_minute` and `ends_minute` on the quarter hour), keyed by `user_id` with row-level security forced to that user and only `authenticated` granted. No row means the default, 09:00 to 17:30 Monday to Friday (`DEFAULT_WORKING_HOURS` in `lib/working-hours.ts`, client-safe). Settings › Calendars saves all seven days at once (`saveWorkingHoursAction`, `lib/crm/working-hours.ts`). Only Plan and Schedule read them: `scheduleChoices` and `nextFreeStart` (`lib/crm/task-time.ts`) take the hours, and `nextFreeAction` returns the next free slot and the morning choices worked out with them. `task-due.ts` keeps its fixed 09:00 to 17:30 and booking pages keep their own hours. A day is off when the hours say so, on a bank holiday or over Christmas (`offDayReason`).

Plan (time blocking Phase 4): `packPlan` (`lib/crm/plan-tasks.ts`, pure and client-safe, tested without a model) places the viewer's own open tasks for today and the next 14 days. It works in instants on a 5 minute grid but takes each day's open and close from London wall-clock working hours, so the clock changes are handled. Busy, tentative and out-of-office meetings block, with 5 minutes after each; free, working-elsewhere, declined and all-day ones don't (`meetingBlocksPlan`). Timed tasks block their own time and are left alone; with Replan my blocks, each future block with `scheduled_by` `planner` is placed first, on its own day only: every such block's current time stays held while the others move; every block whose time still works (no meeting, inside working hours, not in the past) is decided first and seeded as a run, so run limits count it; such a block then only moves to join another run of its group with room, and the rest move, and one that finds no other time keeps its current time as busy and is never listed under Couldn't fit, so nothing is placed over it. Blocks the person dragged in the preview are sent back as `keep` when the plan is made again: they stay at their dragged time, take that time out for everything else, and are only kept for tasks the plan would still place, not in the past, and on the task's own day unless `moveDay` names the new one (a replanned block only on its day). A drag over a planner block that has nowhere else to go leaves both, and the preview shows the drag as clashing with it. Existing timed blocks of a group seed that group's runs, so calls already on the calendar count towards the 6 task and 90 minute limit and the break. Dated tasks are placed next, then undated ones (which first try to join an existing run, then take the earliest day with room). Calls and meeting tasks (`planGroup` over `taskKind`) and emails each form runs: a task joins a run of its group on that day by going straight after or before it, up to 6 tasks or 90 minutes, and a new run keeps 15 minutes from any other run of the same group (an email can sit straight after a call run, meeting gaps aside); other tasks go singly at the earliest free time. A run or task prefers its group's learned times (see Learned times below) or, until there are enough, its default window (`DEFAULT_PLAN_PREFERENCES`: calls 10:30 to 12:30, emails 13:00 to 15:30, other tasks any time; the closest free start wins), and a new run looks for room for the tasks still waiting to join it. Overdue tasks are listed with a suggested slot today or on the next working day with room; a task that couldn't fit gets its reason ("No 2 hour gap on Mon 16 Nov", "Sat 14 Nov is outside your working days", "No room in the next two weeks"), the next working day with room (`nextFree`) and the first free time after hours, or else before them, on its day (`outside`). Every suggestion is held so two never share a slot. `planReason` words the reason line: "Calls late morning and emails after lunch by default." for groups still on their defaults, and "Calls in the morning, as you usually do." (naming where those blocks sit) only for a learned group when at least half its blocks, leaving out ones dragged in the preview, sit in learned periods; a learned group whose blocks couldn't go there gets no line.

Learned times (time blocking Phase 5): `lib/crm/schedule-preferences.ts` learns, per person, when they do calls (with meeting tasks), emails and other tasks (`planGroup` over the task's title), over the last 90 days or since their Reset, whichever is later, across every workspace they're in. It builds a spread over the week's 336 half hours in London wall time (`slotAt` in `lib/learned-times.ts`, client-safe, so the clock change is handled) from three signals, weighted 4, 2 and 1 (`SIGNAL_WEIGHTS`): times they gave their own tasks by hand, meetings they organised with contacts, and their own tasks they ticked off. Times by hand are `task.created` and `task.updated` audit events with the person as `actor_user_id`, `actor_type` `user`, source `web` and operation `create_task`, `update_task` (the task's fields and Schedule) or `drag_task`, on a task they own, whose `after` is timed and `scheduledBy` `person`. Only the latest per task counts, and a time later removed still counts. Moving a block Plan placed to another day at the same London time of day keeps it `planner` (`keepsPlannersTime` in `lib/crm/tasks.ts`, for `updateTask` and `placeTask`; only a new time makes it the person's), and learning also skips any edit whose `before.scheduledBy` was `planner` unless the time of day changed, so Plan's own time never teaches. Ticks are `task.completed` events with operation `complete_task` from `web` on a task they own, bucketed at the event's own `after.completedAt` and only while that still equals the task's `completed_at`. Plan's Apply (`plan_tasks`, and `scheduledBy` `planner`), the assistant and MCP (`schedule_tasks`, and sources `assistant` or `email`), agent tokens (`actor_type` `agent`), `calendar_sync`, Remind me (`snooze_task`) and workflows never teach, so Plan can't teach itself. An agent can't claim the web as its source: `agentSource` (`lib/crm/validation.ts`) drops a provenance source of `web` from the REST API and every MCP tool. It reads one audit query per workspace per run on the existing `audit_events_actor_idx` (`workspace_id, actor_user_id, created_at`), so no new index. Meetings are the person's own `crm.calendar_events` in the window that have `contact_ids`, aren't cancelled, all-day or declined, and were organised by them (`created_by_orbit`, no organiser, or the organiser is the event's account or mailbox address). They count one per `ical_uid`, at their latest start, and then once per subject (trimmed, any case), London time of day and set of contacts, since Microsoft gives each occurrence of a series its own `iCalUId`; so an event on two accounts and a recurring series each count once. They count towards calls. A kind is learned at about ten signals (`LEARN_THRESHOLD`, with a tick counting half). `learnedPeriods` smooths the week's time-of-day profile and takes each run of half hours at half its peak or more (`LEARNED_GOOD`) as a learned period, up to three (`MAX_PERIODS`, the strongest), the same on every working day. Plan, Settings and the assistant all read these periods. The packer's `preferenceScore` (`periodScore`) scores a block 0 only when the whole block sits inside a period, and otherwise 1 plus the minutes to the nearest period, so learned times come first, then the nearest of the rest of working hours; a kind learned wholly outside working hours lands as close to it as they allow. An undated task of a learned kind takes the earliest learned time free in the next 3 working days that still have learned time ahead (`LEARNED_UNDATED_DAYS`; today only counts while some of it is left), and otherwise the earliest day with room as before. The reason line (`planReason`) leaves out blocks dragged in the preview and names where the fitting blocks actually sit ("Calls in the morning or after lunch, as you usually do."), only when at least half of that kind's blocks sit in learned periods and they span no more than two parts of the day. `crm.schedule_preferences` (one row per `user_id`, row-level security forced to that user, `authenticated` granted select, insert and update only) caches the spread (`spread`, `learned_at`) for 10 minutes, so a preview made again reuses it, and holds `reset_at`; a cache write only lands if `reset_at` is unchanged since it was read, so a Reset in flight wins. `readPlanContextInDb` learns on the same client as everything else it reads (`planPreferencesInDb`), inside a savepoint with a 3 second `statement_timeout` (`withinLearningBudget`, which puts the previous timeout back), and Plan uses `DEFAULT_PLAN_PREFERENCES` if learning fails or runs out of time; Settings shows the defaults the same way. An agent's `scheduleTasks` doesn't learn, since it only checks slots. The cache isn't written by MCP's read-only `plan_tasks`, by the assistant's `preferredTimes`, or when the person's workspaces couldn't be read (`learningActors`). Settings › Calendars (`getLearnedTimes`, always fresh) shows a Learned times section, personal and the same in every workspace: per kind, the learned periods or the default (a period longer than 3 hours by its range, such as "09:00 to 17:00", or Any time when it covers most of the person's working hours), a Monday to Friday strip from 08:00 to 18:00 by half hour showing the week-wide pattern Plan uses (faint until a kind is learned), what it learned from, and Reset (`resetLearnedTimesAction`, a hold button) for every kind at once. The assistant's `list_calendar_events` returns `preferredTimes` (`getPreferredTimes`: per kind `learned` and `periods`) for its own `schedule` lists, and MCP's `plan_tasks` uses the same packer and preferences.

Outlook (time blocking Phase 6): a timed task with an owner becomes an event in that owner's main Outlook calendar (the default calendar of their Settings › Email mailbox, `POST /me/events`), built by `taskBlockDraft` (`lib/microsoft/calendar.ts`): the task title, Busy, `sensitivity: private`, reminder off (Orbit sends task reminders), no attendees, no `contact_ids`, and a body of "From Orbit." with an Open the task link (`/open` in the task's own workspace, from `SITE_URL`). So colleagues and booking pages see Busy only, and a Use for CRM calendar never shows it to teammates. The link: `crm.tasks.calendar_event_ref` holds the provider event id, and the event's `crm.calendar_events` row carries `task_id` (`on delete set null`, unique), `block_written_at` and `block_workspace_id` (the workspace its Open the task link points to, so moving the task to another workspace patches the link). A person can only put `task_id` on their own event for a task they own (the insert policy and `calendar_events_task_guard`). Readers that draw or count meetings skip rows with `task_id`: `listCalendarItems` (the grid, the agenda and `list_calendar_events`, which take blocks from the tasks instead, so each block shows once), `isPersonalEvent` (and `move_meeting` and `cancel_meeting` refuse a block's event with "This is a task's block. Move or complete the task instead."), starting soon alerts, `busyFor` (`task-due.ts`), Mail's offered times (`lib/mail/scheduling.ts`), Plan's meetings (so the planner never treats its own blocks as meetings; timed tasks reach it as tasks), People you've met, Capture, `linkEvents` and the hold calendar default. Writing: a trigger on `crm.tasks` (`private.task_outlook_enqueue`) queues the task in `crm.task_outlook_queue` (row-level security forced, no grants; only `private.*` definer functions touch it) whenever its time, duration, title, owner, completion, archive or workspace changes, if it is in Outlook or should be (owned, timed, open). `lib/crm/task-outlook.ts` works the queue: `private.claim_task_blocks` leases up to 50 due tasks for 2 minutes (`for update skip locked`, so two runs never write the same task), `planBlock` decides from the task alone (pure, tested), and `private.settle_task_block` stores the event id and clears the queue entry only if the task hasn't changed since it was claimed. The event id is stored (`private.link_task_block`) straight after Graph creates the event, before its row, so a failed save never leads to a second event. The rules: an owned, timed, open task gets an event if its block hasn't ended, and an existing one is patched by its stored id whenever its time, length or title differ (so an overdue block older than the sync window is moved, never duplicated); Done removes the event when it was completed before the block started, shortens it to the completion minute when completed during the block, and leaves it when completed after; Reopen puts it back if the block hasn't ended; date only, archived or unassigned removes it; an event in someone else's Outlook (the owner changed) is removed first, acting as that person. Each run acts as the event's person through `memberActor`-style membership (`private.memberships_for`, preferring the task's workspace), opens their main connection with `writableSourceToken`, and creates with a `transactionId` derived from the task and its queue time, so a retried create returns the same Outlook event. A write that can't be made keeps the queue entry (`private.defer_task_block`: `attempts` goes up and `retry_at` backs off from 1 minute to 6 hours, only while the entry's `pending_at` still matches the claim, so a newer change is never parked), so while a move, shortening or removal is waiting the task stays queued and read-back leaves it alone; this covers throttling, a 5xx, a network error, a 403, a read-only account and a revoked token alike. A 429 also defers the rest of that mailbox's batch. Only a create without an event can give up: with no write access it is dropped (blocks stay in Orbit only, and a later sync with write access queues it again), a 403 backs off like any other failure (so a long outage only slows retries to every 6 hours), and only a create Graph refuses with a 400 is parked (`refused`, never retried) until the task changes or write access is newly confirmed. Removing an event acts through any workspace its person is still in, so a removed member's block still leaves their Outlook. A 404 on a move creates a fresh event. When the previous owner's Outlook can't be reached during a hand-over, the link is dropped (accepting an orphan event there) and the task is queued again for the new owner. Each run acts as the event's person only in the task's own workspace (`private.memberships_for`); someone who isn't a member there is treated as unreachable. Nothing blocks a response: `queueTaskBlock` (`lib/crm/task-block-queue.ts`) collects the task ids touched in a transaction (`commitBatch` in `lib/crm/after-commit.ts`) and runs them in one `after()` (skipped outside a request), the Done beacon included, so the held Done commits first. `/api/cron/mail-due` works the whole queue every minute, and each calendar sync first queues the person's owned, timed, open tasks without an event and any block whose event differs from its task or should be gone (`private.queue_task_blocks`, only when the mailbox grants `Calendars.ReadWrite`; when `private.note_task_blocks_write` sees that access newly confirmed for the connection it also clears the back-off and parked entries, so reconnecting fills and heals Outlook) and works their queue. A block is only ever written for a time the owner set: when a create or update would leave a task timed and owned by someone other than the acting person, and the write sets its time or its owner, `createTaskInDb` and `applyTask` save it date only on its day (`setsSomeoneElsesTime` and `keepDayOnly`, clearing `scheduledBy` and the reminder, recorded in the audit event; undo restores the time). Agent tokens have no person, so they never set a clock time on an owned task; unowned tasks keep theirs. Undo is exempt only when the restore carries both the time and the owner (a hand-over with its time); an undo that restores only an owner, or only a time onto a task someone else now owns, goes through the rule. Only the owner changes a timed task's length (`resizesSomeoneElsesBlock`, 403). `private.team_remove` hands a removed member's tasks to the remover date only. ClickUp sync records the date-only value in the task's ClickUp snapshot when the rule downgrades a teammate's timed ClickUp task, so the downgrade is never pushed back to ClickUp. Mail Remind me tasks are date only, so they never reach Outlook. Read back: the fetch start is read from the database clock (`clock_timestamp()`), the same clock that stamps `block_written_at`, and every check allows a 2 minute margin (`BLOCK_WRITE_MARGIN_MS`). During a sync of the main connection, `saveEvents` keeps a block row's own time and subject when it was written less than 2 minutes before that fetch began, links a fetched event to its task by `calendar_event_ref` if its row was lost, and neither delete branch removes a row with `task_id`. `readBackTaskBlocks` then compares each of the calendar's block rows with its task, skipping tasks still queued (Orbit's change is newer) and tasks the syncing person doesn't own or whose workspace they've left. A block moved or resized in Outlook moves or resizes the task through `scheduleTaskInDb` with `scheduledBy` `person` (length rounded to whole minutes, 5 to 480); a block deleted in Outlook makes an open task date only on its day and drops the link (for a done task it only drops the link), but only when the fetch was complete, the block starts inside the sync window, Orbit wrote it more than 2 minutes before the fetch began, and `GET /me/events/{id}` returns 404 (an event that only left the fetched range is kept). All-day or cancelled versions are ignored. These run as the owner in the task's workspace, record `task.updated` with provenance `calendar_sync` from `microsoft` (which Learned times never count), and push to ClickUp. Settings › Calendars says under the main account "Task blocks go to your Outlook calendar as Busy, Private.", or, when the account is read only, "Task blocks show in Orbit only." with Reconnect to put task blocks in Outlook. Booking pages: `private.booking_busy` returns the owner's busy events that aren't task blocks, plus their open, timed tasks from every workspace they're in, at the task's own time (done tasks excluded), so a block counts once whether or not it has reached Outlook, a block waiting for a move or removal counts where the task is, and a date-only or done task never counts, through `tasks_owner_timed_idx`.

`buildPlan` reads the viewer's tasks per workspace (`readPlannerTasks`, through `withActor` as each workspace's actor), their working hours and their own calendar events for the window plus 32 days (all calendars, not only visible ones), then orders the tasks with one `completeJson` call (`orderWithAi`: a zod schema, a 20 second deadline, no call without `LLM_API_KEY` or with fewer than two tasks) that returns each task's group and, for a task at the default 15 minutes, a suggested length. `settleOrdering` keeps only known task ids, appends any the model missed in the fallback order (kind, due date, priority, created), rounds lengths to 5 minutes between 5 and 240 and never changes a duration the person set; the packer still places every block and checks each is free, so the model can't double-book, invent a slot or move a day. If the call fails, the fallback order is used. The proposal carries the ordering, so Replan my blocks reuses it without a second call. MCP's `plan_tasks` skips the AI step.

The Calendar page's Plan button (from `md`, hidden on phones) calls `planAction` and keeps the proposal in the browser (`lib/plan-preview.ts`): proposals show dashed on the grid (`GridTaskBlock` with `proposed`), runs of two or more back-to-back calls or emails are outlined with a count, and `PlanPanel` (`components/crm/calendar-plan.tsx`) replaces the left menu (and tucks away quick add and Ask Orbit with `data-quick-add-hide`) with the reason line, Overdue (Move, Leave, each with Undo, and Move all, which skips anything already chosen), Couldn't fit (Next free day, Outside hours, Leave, each with Undo), Placed (by day, with run headings and "Clashes with {meeting}" from the meetings currently loaded), Replan my blocks, any error, the total and Cancel and Apply. Making the plan again (Replan my blocks) carries over removals, choices and drags (`carryOver`); the switch shows the setting of the preview on screen, and a failed refetch keeps that preview. Below `md` the panel hides and the agenda shows "Plan needs a wider screen." with Cancel. While planning, real blocks don't drag; proposals drag and resize through the same `useTaskDrag` hook and change only the preview (a drag to another day sets `moveDay`), and dropping one on the panel or pressing its cross removes it. A dragged proposal is sent with `byHand` and written as placed by the person, as any drag is. Apply sends `{ taskId, at, durationMinutes, moveDay }` for each to `applyPlanAction`, which calls `scheduleTasks` (`lib/crm/plan-schedule.ts`) with provenance `plan_tasks` from `web` and pushes changed tasks to ClickUp after the response.

`scheduleTasks` is shared by Apply, the assistant's `update_task` `schedule` list and MCP's `schedule_tasks`. It reads the entries (up to 100, unique task ids, `at` in London time or an instant, an optional duration and `moveDay`), resolves each task's workspace actor with `actorForTask` (fail closed: a task in a workspace the person isn't in is not found), then writes every entry in one `withActor` transaction as the person, through `scheduleTaskInDb` (`lib/crm/tasks.ts`) with that workspace's actor, so each gets its own `task.updated` audit event in its own workspace and any refusal rolls the whole set back. `scheduleTaskInDb` locks the row, refuses a task outside the actor's workspace, one the person can't change or a done one, then asks `scheduleRefusal` (`lib/crm/plan-tasks.ts`): only the viewer's own tasks; never a block the person placed; not in the past; a dated task keeps its day unless `moveDay` names the new one (from Apply, any of the person's clicks or drags; from an agent, only for an overdue task). Agents (`mode: "agent"`) are also held to working hours on a working day, blocking meetings (`meetingBlocksPlan`), the person's other blocks and the other entries, with meetings read for the whole span of the entries. An overdue block the person placed may move when `moveDay` names the new day. From Apply, an entry the person dragged in the preview carries `byHand` and is written with `scheduled_by` `person`; the rest are `planner`. Workspaces are resolved in one query (`actorsForTasks`, fail closed) before the transaction. Inside it, `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('plan:' || user id))` serialises schedules for the same person, so two agents can't place overlapping blocks at once; the rows are then locked in id order, and an agent's checks read working hours, meetings and blocks on that same client (`readPlanContextInDb`), so a schedule never needs a second pool connection (Vercel's pool has two). Each entry writes `scheduled_by` `planner` through `applyTask`'s `claimSchedule`.

### Meeting invites

`lib/crm/invites.ts` sends, moves, cancels and answers invites for the signed-in user from any of their invite senders (see Invite senders below). Pure rules shared with the browser (durations, limits, the https-only link check, the default slot and 15-minute snapping) live in `lib/invite-rules.ts`; the Graph calls live in `lib/microsoft/calendar.ts`.

- `crm.profiles.meeting_link`: the user's https meeting link (at most 500 characters, checked in `checkMeetingLink` and by the `profiles_meeting_link_https` constraint), edited in Settings, Meeting link.
- `createInvite(actor, input)` validates before any network call: a title (at most 200 characters), `startLocal` `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm` in London time that must exist (times skipped when the clocks go forward are refused), be in the future and within two years, a whole-minute duration from 5 to 480, up to 50 guests (none adds a personal event, below; each a valid address; duplicates merged case-insensitively; the user's own addresses, i.e. sign-in, profile, other emails, mailbox and calendar-account addresses, dropped), notes up to 4000 characters, and `includeMeetingLink` (default: on when a link is saved; asking for it without a link is an error). It then needs a connected mailbox, refuses with "Reconnect Microsoft to send invites" when the stored scopes lack `Calendars.ReadWrite`, and posts to `POST /me/calendars/{default calendar id}/events` (or `POST /me/events` before the calendar list has synced) with `Prefer: outlook.timezone="UTC"` and: `subject`; `body` `{contentType: "HTML"}` holding the notes HTML-escaped with line breaks as `<br>` and `Join: <a href=link>link</a>`; `start` and `end` as London wall-clock times with `timeZone: "GMT Standard Time"` (the Windows name for London; times in the repeated autumn hour, and other app time zones without a mapping, are sent in UTC instead); `location.displayName` set to the link; attendees as `required`; `isOnlineMeeting: false`; `responseRequested: true`; `allowNewTimeProposals: true`; and a `transactionId`. The dialog makes one random UUID per submission and keeps it across retries of that submission; the server retries once, with the same id, after a network error or a 502, 503 or 504, and makes its own id when none is given. Graph sends the invitations itself; that can't be turned off.
- On success the returned event (UTC) is upserted into `crm.calendar_events` with `created_by_orbit = true` on the mailbox's default calendar row (creating the `__default__` row if the list hasn't synced), linked with the usual rules (`linkEvents` limited to that event), and every guest who is an active contact (the contact chosen in the dialog wins over a duplicate with the same email) gets a `meeting` activity "Invited <name> to <subject>" with the London date and time, the link and the notes, `occurred_at` now, and `metadata {source: "orbit", kind: "invite", eventId, providerEventId, startsAt}`. A failure after Graph accepted the event returns a warning instead of an error, so the user isn't tempted to send it twice.
- `cancelInvite(actor, eventId, comment?)` needs the event to be in a calendar of a connected Microsoft account (the main mailbox or a calendar account), not cancelled, not started, and organised by the user (`created_by_orbit`, or the organiser is one of their addresses). It posts `POST /me/events/{id}/cancel` with `{comment}` when there is one (the organiser-only action that notifies attendees; Delete would not), sets `is_cancelled`, and logs "Cancelled <subject>" on each guest contact. A 404 from Graph is treated as already gone; a 400 saying only the organiser can cancel is explained.
- Personal events: `createInvite` with no `attendees` (missing or an empty list; a list holding only the user's own addresses is still refused, and so are `contactIds` without attendees: "Add each guest's email address, or leave contactIds empty for a personal event.") adds an event just for the user. The calendar comes from `pickPersonalCalendar` over `personalCalendars(readInviteSenders(...))`, the Microsoft senders only (the main mailbox and calendar accounts with `can_send`); an SMTP address as `from` is refused with "<address> has no calendar…" and an unknown one is a 404, both with `field: "from"`. The default (`defaultCalendarAddress`) is `defaultInviteAddress` over those calendars with the last used one taken from the user's newest Orbit event with no attendees and no invite identity (events are stored in the calendar's workspace, so this is per user, not per workspace), then `crm.invite_sender_choices`, then the main mailbox. A calendar with a problem throws it, so it never falls back. It posts `personalDraft` (the same body as `holdDraft` without the "Invite sent from" line: no attendees, `showAs: busy`, `responseRequested: false`, the notes, and the link only when `includeMeetingLink` is true) to that calendar, and saves the row with `created_by_orbit` and no invite fields. No iTIP email, no contacts, no `linkEvents`, no activity, and `crm.invite_sender_choices` isn't written. An event is personal (`isPersonalEvent` in `lib/crm/calendar.ts`, used by move, cancel, `listCalendarItems` and the assistant's cards) when Orbit created it, it has no invite identity or uid, no linked contacts and no attendee who isn't the user. Moving one patches it without the message; cancelling one sends `DELETE /me/events/{id}` instead of `/cancel`, and neither logs anything. Guestless events made in Outlook aren't personal in this sense, so they keep the meeting rules. Sync treats them like any other event: they update from Outlook and are deleted when Outlook no longer has them, never kept as missing holds.
- `respondToInvite(actor, {eventId, response, note?, sendResponse?, proposedStartLocal?, proposedDurationMinutes?})` answers a meeting someone else organised: `accept`, `tentative` or `decline`. It needs the event in a calendar of a connected Microsoft account, answered with that account's token, not cancelled, not started, and not organised by the user (refused with "You're the organiser."). It posts `POST /me/events/{id}/accept`, `/tentativelyAccept` or `/decline` with `sendResponse` (default true), `comment` when there is a note, and, for tentative or decline, `proposedNewTime` (London wall-clock, keeping the meeting's length unless a duration is given). A note or a new time without telling the organiser is refused, as Graph would. A 400 about proposals says the organiser doesn't take them. It stores `response_status`, sets `show_as` to tentative for Maybe (busy when accepting a tentative hold), updates the user's own attendee entry, and logs "Accepted", "Tentatively accepted" or "Declined <subject>" on the organiser's contact when the organiser was told. A 404 marks the event cancelled, like move. Sync fills `response_status` from Graph's `responseStatus.response`.
- Errors: 401 asks to connect again, 403 `ErrorAccessDenied` (and similar) asks to reconnect for `Calendars.ReadWrite`, other 403s say the mailbox can't change that calendar, 429 reports Graph's `Retry-After`, a 400 on a new meeting gives Graph's reason and says to change the meeting rather than retry, and anything else is a fixed sentence per action ("Couldn't create the meeting. Try again." and so on), with Graph's text in the server log.
- `listCalendarItems` adds `createdByOrbit`, `personal` (`isPersonalEvent`) and `canCancel` (same rules as above, and at least one guest who isn't the user, or a personal event), plus `organisedByMe`, `myResponse` and `canRespond`, for the event popover. Meeting details shows Accept, Maybe and Decline, an optional note and "Let the organiser know" when `canRespond` is true (`respondToInviteAction`).

UI: `components/crm/invite-dialog.tsx` is shared by the Calendar page (New event in the header, a click on an empty grid slot, New event on an empty agenda day), the contact header (calendar button next to Call in Teams, disabled without an email) and the calendar button above an email in Mail. Its title is New event. With more than one invite sender it shows a From `NativeSelect` (the addresses), starting on `defaultFrom`; a sender with a problem shows the problem as a link to fix it and disables Send. Guests are optional: with none the select is labelled Calendar and lists `calendars` (starting on `defaultCalendar`), Add meeting link starts off, and the button reads Add to calendar instead of Send invites; adding a guest switches back to the senders, and each mode keeps its own choice. Meeting details show Move and Delete event for personal events, without the message to guests. Guests are searched with `searchContactsToFileAction`. Sending shows a notice ("Invite sent to Jane Doe") and refreshes the page. Server actions: `sendInviteAction` and `cancelInviteAction` in `app/(crm)/calendar/actions.ts`, `saveMeetingLinkAction` in `app/(crm)/settings/actions.ts`.

#### Invite senders

`lib/crm/invite-senders.ts` lists every address that can send an invite (`readInviteSenders`), in this order, one entry per address:

- The main mailbox connection (`primary`). Problems: not connected ("Connect Microsoft in Settings › Email to send invites.") or stored scopes without `Calendars.ReadWrite` ("Reconnect Microsoft to send invites.").
- Each Microsoft calendar account with `can_send`. The event goes into that account's default calendar with that account's token. Add mail (`send_account`) now asks for `Calendars.ReadWrite`; `calendar_accounts.calendar_write` records whether the token has it. `storeAccountToken` sets it from the token's scopes whenever an account token is stored (connect, calendar sync, mail sync and invite refreshes; `coalesce`, so a downgraded scope sets false and a response without scopes keeps it). Null means not known yet. Problems: disconnected ("Reconnect <address> in Settings › Calendars to send invites from it."), `calendar_write` not true ("Allow invites for <address> in Settings › Calendars first.", where Allow invites reruns Add mail, shown in Settings › Calendars whenever `calendar_write` isn't true), or no writable default calendar. The account's stored write flag stands in for its scopes, so a cached token isn't refreshed just to learn them. Allow invites keeps the `send_account` scopes: these accounts already granted `Mail.Send` and `Mail.ReadWrite`, so Microsoft only asks for the calendar permission, and asking for less could leave the stored token without the mail scopes.
- Each SMTP sending address (`crm.send_identities`, `kind: "smtp"`). It needs a hold calendar: `send_identities.hold_calendar_id` when it is still a writable calendar of the main mailbox or a usable calendar account, else the default calendar (main mailbox or calendar account) whose domain best matches (the same domain, or a shared run of at least four letters such as ridgeon in markridgeon.com). With no match the address shows "Choose a hold calendar for <address> in Settings › Email." rather than holding in the main calendar, and a hold calendar whose account hasn't allowed invites shows that account's Allow invites problem. Settings › Email, Sending addresses, has an "Invites hold time in" select per address (Best match or a calendar; `PATCH /api/mail/identities`).

Default From (`defaultInviteAddress`, per active workspace): the first sender whose home (`addressHomesInDb` in `lib/mail/routing.ts`: an explicit `crm.mail_address_homes` row, else the workspace the mail source, sending address or calendar account with mail was added in) is this workspace, explicit homes first, else the last address used for an invite in this workspace (`crm.invite_sender_choices`, written after every successful `createInvite`), else the main mailbox, else the first sender without a problem. `pickInviteSender` takes the requested `from` (an address, case-insensitive) or that default. It never falls back: an unknown address is a 404 "<address> can't send invites. Choose another From address.", and a sender with a problem throws that problem with `field: "from"`, so the dialog and the assistant say exactly what to reconnect.

`getInviteSetup` adds `senders` (`{address, kind, primary, problem, fixHref}`), `defaultFrom`, and `calendars` and `defaultCalendar` for personal events; `ownEmails` includes the SMTP addresses, so they are dropped from guest lists.

Microsoft senders work as above with the sender's token and default calendar; the saved event keeps `account_id` (or `connection_id`), so move, cancel and respond later use the same account. Orbit-created events on a calendar account are linked to contacts even when that calendar isn't used for CRM.

SMTP senders (`lib/ical/itip.ts`): Orbit first creates the hold in the hold calendar (`holdDraft`: same subject, times, location and notes, a line "Invite sent from <address> by Orbit.", no attendees so Outlook sends nothing, `showAs: busy`, `responseRequested: false`, the same `transactionId`). It then builds an iTIP email with nodemailer's `MailComposer`: `multipart/mixed` holding `multipart/alternative` (plain text and HTML in Orbit's email style: "<name> invited you to <title>." then When, Join, Organiser and Guests, then the notes) with a `text/calendar; method=REQUEST` part, plus `invite.ics`. The calendar has `METHOD:REQUEST`, one `VEVENT` with `UID:orbit-<transactionId>@<sender domain>` (stable across retries of one submission), `SEQUENCE:0`, `DTSTAMP`, UTC `DTSTART`/`DTEND`, `SUMMARY`, `DESCRIPTION` (notes and Join link), `LOCATION` and `URL` (the link), `ORGANIZER;CN=<name>:mailto:<address>`, an `ATTENDEE;CN=…;CUTYPE=INDIVIDUAL;ROLE=REQ-PARTICIPANT;PARTSTAT=NEEDS-ACTION;RSVP=TRUE` per guest, `STATUS:CONFIRMED` and `TRANSP:OPAQUE`; text is escaped and lines are folded at 75 octets. It goes out through the address's SMTP server (`defaultSmtpSender`, injectable as `smtp`) and is appended to its IMAP Sent folder when it has one. If sending fails, the hold is deleted, `send_identities.last_error` is set and the error names the address. The Orbit row is the hold (`calendar_id` and `account_id` or `connection_id` of the hold calendar) with `organizer` set to the SMTP address, the guests as attendees, `invite_identity_id`, `invite_uid`, `invite_sequence` and `invite_notes`. Calendar sync keeps `organizer`, `attendees` and `response_status` on rows with `invite_identity_id`, so the hold syncs without losing the guests. Free/busy, booking pages (`private.booking_busy`) and other people's Outlook see the hold.

Moving an SMTP invite patches the hold (without the message), or makes a new hold with `holdDraft` when it is marked missing or the patch gets a 404 (the row keeps its id and gets the new `provider_event_id`; it is never marked cancelled), then sends a `REQUEST` with `SEQUENCE + 1`, the new times, the original notes and an "Updated invitation" email naming the old time and the message; if the email fails the hold is moved back. Cancelling sends a `CANCEL` (`STATUS:CANCELLED`, attendees `PARTSTAT=DECLINED`, `SEQUENCE + 1`) and then deletes the hold (`DELETE /me/events/{id}`; if that fails the guests were still told and a warning says to delete the hold in Outlook). `invite_sequence` is bumped in the database before each `REQUEST` or `CANCEL` is sent and put back if the send fails. Calendar sync never deletes a row with `invite_identity_id` or `invite_uid` whose hold has gone from Outlook: it sets `invite_hold_missing` instead (cleared when the hold syncs again), cancel skips deleting a missing hold, and move recreates it. A row whose sending address was removed (`invite_uid` set, `invite_identity_id` null) can't be moved or cancelled, and `removeIdentity` refuses while the address has upcoming invites that aren't cancelled ("Cancel the 2 upcoming meetings sent from <address> first…"). Removing an address or disconnecting a calendar account also clears it from `booking_pages.invite_sender` and `crm.invite_sender_choices`.

Replies (`lib/mail/invite-replies.ts`): the mail cron reads, for each SMTP address with an invite that hasn't ended, the address's IMAP inbox (Neo has one) with IMAP timeouts set from the deadline. It resumes after `send_identities.reply_last_uid` while `reply_uid_validity` matches the mailbox's UIDVALIDITY (300 messages a run); the first run, or a run after UIDVALIDITY changes, reads from the day before the first open invite (at most 14 days back, the last 300 messages). It downloads `text/calendar` and `application/ics` parts (at most 256 KB each, `maxBytes`) with the message's From and Sender, and applies `METHOD:REPLY` ones (`readItipReply`) whose `UID` matches `invite_uid` and whose `ORGANIZER` is the sending address, taking only the `ATTENDEE` entries whose address is the message's From or Sender, so a forged reply can't answer for someone else. Replies to an older `SEQUENCE` are ignored and a reply without one counts as current: `ACCEPTED`, `TENTATIVE` and `DECLINED` become the guest's `accepted`, `tentativelyAccepted` and `declined`. Without an IMAP server, responses aren't read.

Booking pages send from `booking_pages.invite_sender`. Null means the booking default (`bookingDefaultSender`): the address homed in the booking page's workspace, else the main mailbox, else the same fallback as the dialog. Choosing the default stores null. Invites from in Settings › Booking page shows when there is more than one sender and starts on the default; a saved address that can no longer send is dropped from the form with a warning. A booking whose saved address isn't a sender any more fails with "<address> can't send invites any more. Choose who booking invites come from in Settings › Booking page." as the page's last error.

Migration `20270104100000_invite_senders.sql` adds `calendar_events.invite_identity_id`, `invite_uid`, `invite_sequence` and `invite_notes`, `send_identities.hold_calendar_id`, `calendar_events.invite_hold_missing` (and update grants on it and `provider_event_id`), `send_identities.reply_uid_validity` and `reply_last_uid`, `booking_pages.invite_sender`, `calendar_accounts.calendar_write`, and the owner-only `crm.invite_sender_choices` (one row per user and workspace, RLS forced). It can be run more than once.

Migration `20261210110000_invite_response.sql` adds `calendar_events.response_status`. Migration `20261024100000_meeting_invites.sql` adds `profiles.meeting_link`, `calendar_events.created_by_orbit` and `integration_state.granted_scopes`. It doesn't touch `crm.mailbox_connections`, `crm.email_messages` or `private.schema_migrations`, so it runs in the hosted SQL editor as `postgres`, and it can be run more than once.

### Booking page

A public scheduling page at `/book/[slug]` (`app/book/[slug]`, outside the `(crm)` group, so no CRM chrome). Each person has at most one page per workspace (unique on `user_id, workspace_id`; the slug stays unique across Orbit, checked by `private.booking_slug_taken(slug, user, workspace)`). Settings, the assistant's links, `[[times]]` and the cold email playbook read the page for the current workspace and give no link when it has none. Settings › Booking page edits only that page; there is no copying from another workspace's page, and a workspace without one starts from the defaults. `proxy.ts` lets `/book` and `/book/*` through without a session; `/api/*` was already public at the proxy and each route authenticates itself.

| Table | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `crm.booking_pages` | One row per user (`user_id` unique): `slug` (unique, `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, 3 to 40 characters, reserved words refused by `booking_pages_slug_reserved`, kept in step with `RESERVED_SLUGS` in `lib/booking/rules.ts` by a test), `enabled` (default off), `title` (null means "Book a call with <first name>"), `intro` (up to 500), `duration_minutes` (15, 20, 30, 45, 60), `buffer_minutes` (0 to 60), `min_notice_minutes` (0 to 14 days), `horizon_days` (1 to 90), `weekly_hours` (`{"mon": [{"start": "09:00", "end": "17:30"}], …}`, checked by `private.booking_hours_valid`), `time_zone` (Europe/London), `background_video_url` (https, up to 1000), `background_poster` and `avatar` (bytea up to 1 MB, JPEG, PNG or WebP, with their mime types), `last_error` and `last_error_at` (why the last booking failed, shown in Settings), and timestamps. Owner-only RLS for `authenticated` (`private.session_user_id()` and the workspace check); agents and anonymous users have no access. |

Public access only goes through security-definer functions executable by `crm_app` alone: `private.booking_page_public(slug)` returns the page fields a visitor needs (no email, meeting link, workspace or image bytes; `meeting_kind` is just `whereby`, `link` or null, and `version` is a hash of `updated_at` for image URLs) for enabled pages only; `private.booking_page_image(slug, kind)` returns the avatar or poster bytes; `private.booking_busy(user_id, from, to)` returns busy intervals from `crm.calendar_events` across all the user's calendars (not cancelled, `show_as` not `free`, at most a 100-day window) and only for a user with an enabled page; `private.booking_page_owner(slug)` returns the owner's membership row, from which `memberActor` builds the background actor that does the booking; `private.booking_slug_taken(slug, user_id)` backs the live availability check in Settings. `lib/booking/pages.ts` wraps them and the owner's settings reads and writes; `publicView` is the only shape sent to the browser.

Slots (`lib/booking/slots.ts`, pure, also used by the browser for month maths): for each local date in the window, each weekly range, every `duration` minutes for 15, 20 and 30-minute meetings and every 30 minutes for longer ones, wall times are converted with `zonedTimeToDate`; times that don't exist when the clocks go forward are skipped, a range ending at `24:00` ends at the next midnight, and a slot must end by the range's end. A slot is offered when it starts at or after now plus the minimum notice, before the start of the local day `horizon_days + 1` days from today, and doesn't come within `buffer` minutes of a busy interval on either side. Results are UTC instants. `GET /api/book/[slug]/slots?month=YYYY-MM&tz=Area/City` returns `{month, timeZone, first, last, days}` with the slots of that month grouped by the visitor's local date (an unknown zone falls back to the page's); the panel defaults `tz` to the browser zone and offers every `Intl.supportedValuesOf("timeZone")`.

Booking (`POST /api/book/[slug]`, `bookSlot` in `lib/booking/book.ts`): rate limits through `private.consume_rate_limit` with HMAC-hashed keys (`booking:slots:<ip>` 10 a minute, `booking:attempts:<ip>` 5 an hour, `booking:email:<email>` 3 a day; the IP is the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry, which Vercel sets); a filled hidden `website` field is refused; name (1 to 120), email (`canonicalEmail`) and note (up to 1000) are validated; the start must be one of the generated slots against stored busy data. It then takes a per-owner session advisory lock (`pg_advisory_lock(hashtextextended('booking:<user_id>', 0))`) on a dedicated connection (`openClient`, so a waiting request doesn't hold a pool slot), re-reads busy data, opens the owner's sender (`openInviteSender` with the page's Invites from address, or the booking default: `Calendars.ReadWrite`, fresh token), refuses the organiser's own addresses, and reads Graph `calendarView` of the sender's calendar (the hold calendar for an SMTP sender) from start minus buffer to end plus buffer; a Graph failure there falls back to stored data. A clash is a 409 "That time was just taken. Pick another.". The meeting goes through `sendInvite` (the second half of `createInvite`) as the owner: subject "<owner first name> × <visitor name>", body "Note from <name>:" plus the note and the Join link, location the meeting link, the visitor as the only required attendee, in the page's time zone. With `createContacts` it calls `createContactFromAttendeeInDb` in a savepoint (internal addresses are skipped), saves the event with `created_by_orbit`, links it, and logs a `meeting` activity "Booked via your booking page" with `metadata {source: "booking", kind: "booking", eventId, providerEventId, startsAt, page}`, all before the lock is released. Not connected, missing write scope, or a Graph error are logged in `booking_pages.last_error` and shown to the visitor only as a 503 "Booking is unavailable right now"; a successful booking clears the error. After the booking is saved, `alertOwner` (in `after()`, inline outside a request) adds a `booking` notification linking to the contact and sends the owner a plain-text "New booking" email from their own mailbox to itself through `sendOwnMail`; either failing is logged and never affects the booking.

UI: `components/booking/booking-screen.tsx` (panel, slots, form, confirmation), `booking-calendar.tsx` (month grid with arrow-key, Home and End navigation), `booking-background.tsx` (fixed `<video autoplay muted loop playsinline>` with the poster, faded in once playing and dropped on error or for `prefers-reduced-motion`, otherwise a CSS-only aurora whose keyframes live in `globals.css` and stop under reduced motion), and `components/crm/booking-page-settings.tsx`. Images are served by `GET /api/book/[slug]/image/[kind]` (public, cached, `nosniff`) and `/api/booking-page/image` (owner: GET, multipart POST, DELETE; same-origin check; type from the file's magic bytes). Videos are only stored as links: a Vercel function response is capped at 4.5 MB and Postgres is the wrong place for large media. The page sets `robots: noindex` on top of the site-wide `robots.txt`.

Migration `20261026100000_booking_pages.sql` creates the table, policies and functions. It doesn't touch `crm.mailbox_connections`, `crm.email_messages` or `private.schema_migrations`, re-creates `private.session_user_id()`, and can be run more than once.

### ClickUp

`lib/clickup.ts` is the API v2 client (personal token in `Authorization` without a scheme, `CLICKUP_API_TOKEN`, base `CLICKUP_API_BASE_URL`): user, workspaces, the space › folder › list hierarchy (cached five minutes per process), list statuses and custom fields, task create, update and get, the filtered workspace task search, custom field values, and webhooks. On 429 it waits until `X-RateLimit-Reset` (at most 15 seconds) only when the request deadline leaves room, otherwise it gives up for this run. `lib/crm/clickup.ts` holds the sync.

| Column | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `crm.tasks.clickup_task_id` | The linked ClickUp task, unique per workspace when set. |
| `crm.tasks.clickup_list_id` | The list the task lives in. Set without a task id, it means "create this task in ClickUp" and the sync keeps trying until it succeeds. |
| `crm.tasks.clickup_url`, `clickup_synced_at`, `clickup_error` | The ClickUp link, the last successful exchange, and the last error shown on the Orbit task. |
| `crm.tasks.clickup_sync` | The last values both sides agreed on (`title`, `dueAt`, `dueHasTime`, `done`; a snapshot without `dueHasTime` compares the instant only), ClickUp's `date_updated` at that point, when Orbit last wrote to ClickUp (`pushedAt`), and a short-lived claim while the task is being created (`creatingAt`). |
| `crm.integration_state` (`source = 'clickup'`) | One row per Orbit workspace, so each workspace connects and chooses its own lists with the one token: the member it runs as, the ClickUp workspace (`team_id`), the token owner's ClickUp user id, the default list, the watched lists, the last list used in a form, the list names for the pickers (`list_choices`), the webhook id, its secret (encrypted with `APP_SECRET`) and scope (`team`, `space:<id>`, or `list:<id>`), the last sync, and the last error. |
| `crm.webhook_receipts` (`source = 'clickup'`) | `webhook_id:history item ids` (or `webhook_id:event:task_id` for deletions) seen in the last day. |

Merging is three-way against `clickup_sync`. A field Orbit changed since the last agreement is sent to ClickUp; a field ClickUp changed is written to the Orbit task through `updateTaskInDb`, with the audit source `clickup`; when both changed, the later of the task's `updated_at` and ClickUp's `date_updated` wins. A ClickUp task whose `date_updated` is not newer than the stored one is skipped, so Orbit's own writes coming back through the poll or the webhook change nothing, and a webhook event dated within 15 seconds of Orbit's last write to that task is dropped without fetching it. Imported tasks are created with `createTaskInDb`; while an Orbit-created task is being sent (`creatingAt` within two minutes), a ClickUp task in the same list with the same title is not imported, so the webhook for Orbit's own create cannot make a copy. Due dates are London time. A date-only Orbit task goes out as London midnight with `due_date_time: false` and a timed one with `due_date_time: true` (`toClickUpDue` reads `due_has_time`); from ClickUp, a due with a time makes a timed task, and `due_date_time: false`, or a time of exactly 00:00 or 04:00 London when the flag is missing, makes a date-only task at midnight. Done means a status of type `done` or `closed`.

Each sync claims the row with a five-minute gap (the cron and Sync force it), lists tasks in the watched lists and in lists that hold linked tasks, updated since the last complete sync less five minutes (14 days the first time), 100 a page, closed tasks and subtasks included, then sends up to 20 pending or changed tasks, then re-reads up to five linked open tasks not checked for six hours, archiving any whose ClickUp task is gone. Running out of time keeps `last_synced_at` where it was. `private.clickup_webhook_targets()` returns the stored webhook secrets for the public route, `/api/clickup/webhook`; it is a security-definer function executable only by `crm_app`.

Workspaces: the token (`CLICKUP_API_TOKEN`) is shared, while the ClickUp workspace, default list, watched lists, webhook and sync state are per Orbit workspace, so Eclipse tasks and Mark Ridgeon tasks go to different lists. A push (`pushClickUpTask`, `sendTaskToDefaultList`) runs as the person's actor for the task's own workspace (`taskWorkspaceActor`) and reads the task only within it, so a follow-up created in another workspace is sent to that workspace's list. Each workspace registers its webhook at `/api/clickup/webhook?workspace=<id>` and only replaces its own, so turning on instant sync in one workspace leaves the other's in place. Saving lists that another of the person's workspaces also watches or uses as its default says so (`sharedLists`), because tasks in a list both watch sync into both. The settings card names the workspace the lists are for.

### Meta lead ads

`app/api/webhooks/meta-leads/route.ts` hands `GET` to `handleMetaVerification` and `POST` to `handleMetaLeadsWebhook` in `lib/crm/meta-leads.ts`; `lib/meta.ts` is the Graph API client (one version constant, `META_GRAPH_VERSION`, default `v27.0`; the token goes in the `Authorization` header, never the URL; `META_GRAPH_URL` may only point at localhost; timeouts are whole milliseconds; errors go through `lib/upstream.ts`, logged with secrets redacted; paging stays on the Graph origin; `cut` repairs broken characters and then shortens text to the same UTF-16 length the record validators use, never splitting a pair). The request is rate limited per address (`private.consume_rate_limit`), capped at 1 MB while it is read, and its `X-Hub-Signature-256` checked over the raw bytes in constant time before anything is parsed. Large numeric ids are quoted before `JSON.parse` so they stay exact, and only `leadgen` changes are read; everything else in the body is ignored. Every lead ID is saved in one call (`private.meta_leads_receive`) before the 200, and a failure there answers 503 so Meta retries. The Graph fetch and filing run in `after()`, one lead at a time, each in its own try so one bad lead can't stop the rest. Failures are logged with the lead ID and the error type only.

Three tables, all with forced row-level security. `crm.meta_lead_pages` maps a Facebook Page ID (unique across Orbit) to one workspace, with who linked it, `full_history` (set by `private.meta_lead_page_linked` only when the linker sees held leads; a session can't set it), and the hourly check's `last_run_at`, `last_attempt_at`, `last_error` and `pending` (not finished); members read it, owners and admins (`private.is_workspace_admin`) add and remove rows, and the insert check requires `linked_by` to be the session user. `crm.meta_lead_forms` keeps each form's read cursor, last error and, while a listing is part read, the Graph paging cursor (`list_since`, `list_after`, `list_started_at`), with no grants. `crm.meta_leads` holds one row per Meta lead ID (`meta_leads_unique`): the answers (`answers`, a jsonb array of `{ name, label, values }`), the form, ad, ad set and campaign ids and names, `platform` (Facebook or Instagram), `is_organic`, `lead_created_at`, the raw webhook change and Graph lead (`payload`), the source "Meta Lead Ad", how it arrived, `held_at` and `released_to`, the contact, company and activity it was filed on, and `fetched_at`, `filed_at`, `notified_at`, `attempts`, `next_attempt_at` and `error`. Owners and admins of its workspace can read it and update only the filing columns, and a trigger (`private.meta_lead_same_workspace`) keeps those links inside the lead's workspace, checking only a link that is being set to a new value; nobody inserts or deletes through a session.

Which workspace a lead belongs to is worked out again for every unfiled lead whenever it is saved or retried (`private.meta_lead_resolve`). A lead goes to the Page's linked workspace when it was released there (`released_to`), or when it isn't held and either the link has `full_history` or the lead was created at or after the link; otherwise it is held (`held_at`). Removing a Page holds its unfiled leads and deletes its form cursors in the same transaction (the `meta_lead_pages_removed` trigger, `private.meta_lead_page_removed`). Held Pages (`private.meta_held_pages`), the "Lead from a Page that isn't linked" alert (`private.meta_lead_alert_owners`, in each person's home workspace) and releasing (`private.meta_release_held`) are only for `private.meta_held_audience()`: people who own every workspace (`private.meta_owns_every_workspace`), or the owners of the oldest workspace when there are none. Linking a Page as one of them releases its held leads and files them straight away; a release to a workspace stands, so relinking the Page there later files them too. A conflict says the Page is linked to another workspace only when the person can see that link under row-level security.

Filing (`fileMetaLead`) runs as one owner or admin of the workspace (the Page's linker when they still are one) inside `withActor`: it locks the lead row (`for update`) so two deliveries can't both file it, finds or creates the company and contact through `lib/crm/companies.ts` and `lib/crm/contacts.ts` under their advisory locks, each in a savepoint so a value that fails validation drops the company or the extra fields instead of the lead, writes the note with `createActivityInDb` (its `metadata.source` is `meta_lead_ad`) and a `lead.created` audit event, and sets `filed_at`. An existing company is never changed by a lead. The provenance is `{ operation: "meta_lead_ad", source: "meta_lead_ad", automatic: true }`, so workflow events don't count the records as the filer's own. After the commit, owners, admins and the linker are alerted in parallel: the `crm.push_log` key and the bell notification (`source = 'meta_lead'`) are written in one transaction, then the push is sent but not waited for past the deadline. `notified_at` is set when every recipient's notification is in; otherwise `private.meta_lead_failed` backs the lead off and the retry alerts again, including leads filed with no details. With under 3 seconds left the alert is left to the retry. A failed fetch or filing records the error and sets `next_attempt_at` (5 minutes doubling to a day for a lead with a workspace, an hour without counting for a held one); nothing is ever given up.

`handleCronRequest` asks `private.meta_lead_work_due` first (three indexed `exists` checks matching the retry queues, plus due Pages). Only when a Page is due or a lead is waiting does `runScheduledSync` get 40 seconds instead of 50, followed by `runMetaLeadBackup` until 50 seconds after the request arrived (at most 10, skipped under 5). The backup reads due Pages (`private.meta_lead_pages_due`: an hour since the last attempt, or `pending`, oldest attempt first), then spends all its time on waiting leads when none is due and 40% otherwise. `private.meta_leads_waiting` returns three queues with their own limits (leads with a workspace, then filed leads not yet notified, then a tenth as many held leads still to be fetched; held leads waiting only for a link aren't returned; after picking, the Page's other due held leads are put back an hour). The `meta_leads_filed` trigger resets `attempts` and `next_attempt_at` when a lead is filed, so its alerts start on a fresh clock. Each Page: `/{page}/leadgen_forms` with the Page token when the System User token can get one, then, least recently attempted form first, `/{form}/leads` filtered on `time_created` from the form's cursor less two hours but never before the link (or a day before it with `full_history`), one Graph page at a time. After each page the paging cursor is saved (`private.meta_lead_form_progress`), and a later run carries on from it; the form's cursor moves only when the listing ends (`private.meta_lead_form_ran`), which also clears the saved paging cursor on any failure other than Orbit's own deadline; a paging cursor over an hour old isn't used. A failing form is recorded and the others still run; a Graph call cut by Orbit's own deadline leaves the Page `pending` with "Not finished" rather than an error. The assistant's web gate treats a tool result holding a record whose `source` is `meta_lead_ad` or "Meta Lead Ad" as outside content and never takes trusted hosts from such a record (`outsideRead` and `firstPartyHosts` in `lib/assistant/web-gate.ts`), the page note leaves out a lead company's domain (`lib/assistant/page-context.ts`), and Orbit Agent jobs mark the read the same way (`noteRead` in `lib/companion/jobs.ts`). Migration `20270112900000_meta_lead_ads.sql`.

## Enrichment, lists and the activity pad

### Enrichment

`lib/enrichment/providers.ts` holds one adapter per provider, `settings.ts` the workspace order and availability, `contacts.ts` and `companies.ts` the lookups, and `limits.ts` the rate limit.
