For a task that is the assignee, the creator, owners and admins, and anyone tagged in it; for a doc, every member of its workspace while it is shared. A private doc refuses with "Share this doc with the workspace to discuss it." and a daily note with "Daily notes don't have a discussion." Unsharing a doc or removing someone from a task takes the discussion away at once, because every read goes through the conversation's select policy. A task moved to another workspace takes its discussion with it: it is then visible to whoever can see the task there (participants who aren't members of the new workspace are dropped by `private.task_participants_move`). An assignee who leaves the workspace but still owns the task loses it too, because every audience check joins `crm.workspace_members`.

Messages, edits, deletes, threads and thread read marks use the Chat service and routes unchanged. `readConversation` falls back to the subject query (`SUBJECT_SQL`, named after the task title or doc title, never unread), and `memberIds` adds the subject's audience (`SUBJECT_AUDIENCE_SQL`, read as the sender: the people above who are still workspace members), so live `chat` events reach everyone who can see the subject and no one else. Discussions never appear in the Chat sidebar, unread counts or the Threads view.

| Call | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/chat/discussions?task=<id>` or `?doc=<id>` (`getDiscussion`) | `ChatDiscussion`: `subject`, `conversationId` (null until the first message), `count` (messages that aren't deleted, replies included) and the first `ChatPage` of root messages. |
| `openDiscussionAction(subject)` (`app/(crm)/discussions.ts`) | `{ conversationId }`. The panel calls it before its first send, then posts with `postMessageAction`. |
| `discussionCounts(actor, "task", ids)` | Counts per task for the Tasks list, read as the person. |

**Tagging.** In a task discussion, `postMessage` and `editMessage` find the newly mentioned people who can't already see the task (not the assignee, creator, an owner or admin, or someone tagged) and pass them to `addTaskParticipantsInDb` before the message is written (provenance `{ operation: "tag_task", source: "chat" }`, with `{ alert: false }`), so it records `task.participant_added`, the person gains sight of the task, its notes and its discussion, and the follow trigger can add them to the thread. Someone outside the task's workspace is refused with "Tag people in this task's workspace." and nothing is saved, the message included. The alerts are sent after the live broadcast: newly tagged people get "Mark Ridgeon tagged you in a task" (linked to `/tasks?who=tagged&task=<id>`), and people who could already see the task get "Mark Ridgeon mentioned you in a task" (linked to `/tasks?task=<id>`), both keyed per message. A mention gives no access, so someone mentioned who later loses sight of the task (unassigned, untagged, or out of the workspace) keeps a bell row that opens the Tasks list with "Task not found."; this is accepted. The `message` live event for a discussion also carries `subject` (`{ kind, id }`), so a panel that has no conversation yet adopts the new one without refetching on every Chat message. In a doc discussion a mention of a workspace member alerts "Sam mentioned you in a doc" (no body, linked to `/docs/<id>?discussion=1`); anyone else is ignored. A reply in a followed thread alerts "Mark Ridgeon replied in a thread on a task" or "Sam replied in a thread on a doc" once per unread burst, like Chat, through `private.chat_subject_followers(user, root)`, which keeps only followers who can still see the subject (`private.chat_subject_open_to`). These alerts use the `task` and `doc` sources, so they land in the subject's workspace. Task alerts use the sender's full name like the other task alerts, doc alerts the first name like doc tags.

**Panels.** `useDiscussion(kind, id, viewer)` (`components/chat/use-discussion.ts`) loads the discussion, keeps it in the Chat reducer, listens to the person channel, and supplies a `ChatContext`, so `components/chat/discussion.tsx` reuses the Chat message row, composer (with the `@` picker), edit, delete and Reply in thread. Threads open in place inside the panel with a back button. On Tasks, a row expands in place (`components/beautifui/action-task.tsx`) and Open shows the task panel (`components/crm/task-panel.tsx`, a `Sheet`, full screen on phones. On Tasks the panel is non-modal on a desktop (wide screen with hover) and a real modal (focus trap, `aria-modal`, overlay) on phones and tablets; the choice is made when the panel opens and kept while it stays open, so crossing the breakpoint never remounts it. `Sheet` passes `modal` to `SheetContent` through context so the Root and the Content always agree. A non-modal sheet has no overlay, outside clicks don't close it and it carries `data-nonmodal`, which the shortcuts provider ignores and which quick add and the Ask Orbit dock measure to move left of it. Record pages keep it modal so the record's `E` is protected). The expanded row and the panel share their fields through `components/crm/task-fields.tsx` (`useTaskDetails` and `TaskFields`), and both complete through `useTaskDone` (`components/crm/use-task-done.ts`), which hides the row at once and shows "Task done." with Undo, but holds `completeTaskAction` until the toast expires, so an undone Done writes nothing. Leaving the list in the app sends it at once (and the toast goes); closing the tab, `pagehide` or the page going hidden (a phone backgrounding Safari) sends the pending ids with `navigator.sendBeacon` (or `fetch` with `keepalive`) to `POST /api/tasks/complete` (`app/api/tasks/complete/route.ts`, logic in `lib/crm/task-beacon.ts`), which needs the session cookie and a same-origin request and completes through `completeTask` as the user with web provenance. `completeTask` locks the row and does nothing to a task that is already done, so a beacon and a later normal send record one `task.completed` and fire one webhook, alert and workflow. Every other route is idempotent too: in `applyTask`, `completed: true` on a done task or `completed: false` on an open one sets nothing and records no audit event (REST `PATCH` and `/complete`, MCP `update_next_action` and `complete_next_action`, the assistant's `update_task`, workflows), still refusing someone who can't change the task; `reopenTask` does nothing to an open or archived task. The toast stays while the page is away; when it comes back (`visibilitychange` or `pageshow`) the list refreshes and `openTaskIdsAction` checks the sent ids, putting back any still open with "Couldn't mark it done. Try again.", and Undo on a sent id calls `reopenTaskAction` (`reopenTask`, audited as `task.reopened`; it refuses people who can't change the task and does nothing to an open or archived one). Record pages use the same rows through `components/crm/record-tasks.tsx`, without row shortcuts. **Acting from a task.** `TASK_SQL` (`lib/crm/tasks.ts`) also reads the contact's job title, phone and email and the company's own phone, since those are shared records; nothing from Mail is joined in. `components/crm/task-actions.tsx` (`TaskAct`, in the expanded row and the panel) orders the actions by `taskKind(title)` (`lib/crm/task-kind.ts`, a pure word match: call, email, meeting or todo) and loads `taskContextAction` (`app/(crm)/task-act.ts`, logic in `lib/crm/task-act.ts`) when the row opens. `taskContext` runs as the viewer through `withActor`: the opportunity, the last activity and the company's people come from shared tables, while the last email and the linked thread (parsed from the task's `/mail?thread=` line) are read from `crm.mail_messages` with `user_id` set to the viewer, so another person's mail never shows and Reply on thread appears only for the viewer's own thread. The viewer's booking link comes from `getBookingPage`. Call uses `teamsCallUrl` on a fine pointer and `tel:` on a coarse one (two links swapped by CSS); Email and Use template open `openComposer`; Invite loads `getInviteSetup` through `taskInviteSetupAction` only when pressed and opens `InviteDialog`. Find phone and Find email are `FindDetailButton` (`components/crm/find-detail.tsx`, the contact page's lookup through `POST /api/enrichment/contacts`), shown beside "No number" or "No email" only when a provider for that detail is configured. The `(crm)` layout reads `enrichmentAvailability()` once per page and passes it down through `FindAvailabilityProvider` (`components/crm/find-availability.tsx`), so opening a row makes no extra request; `taskContext` also returns the contact's latest phone and email lookups from `crm.enrichment_lookups` (read as the viewer), so Not found persists. A found detail refreshes the page. When no provider is configured for a missing detail, or its lookup found nothing, More actions offers Ask Orbit to find details, which dispatches the `orbit:ask` window event (`components/assistant/ask-orbit.ts`) so the dock opens with the request in its input; nothing is sent. Picking a company's person and Edit links call `relinkTask`, which locks the task, checks `canChangeTask`, records nothing when the links are unchanged, and otherwise passes exactly the chosen links (a person or opportunity brings its company) to `updateTaskInDb`, so `resolveLinks` refuses links that don't fit instead of any being dropped, and the change is a `task.updated` audit event with `link_task` provenance. The last email is looked up in two steps, because the line loads every time a row opens and a whole-mailbox scan for someone never emailed would make that slow for a large mailbox (a time budget, not a cap on what is read). First it walks the viewer's own 2,000 most recent messages from the past 12 months, newest first on the `(user_id, received_at)` index, matching From, To or Cc and leaving out trash, junk, outbox and drafts (2.5 ms locally with 60,000 messages and no match). If that finds nothing, it searches the whole of the viewer's mailbox the same way inside a savepoint with an 800 ms `statement_timeout`, so an older email is still found, and a lookup that runs out of time shows no Last email rather than holding the row up. No index was added: the address sits in `jsonb` recipient lists, and a new index on `crm.mail_messages`, a large and busy table, isn't cheap. **Done with an outcome.** On a linked task, the screens (`actions-screen.tsx`, `record-tasks.tsx`) route Done (row or panel) through `useTaskOutcome` (`components/crm/task-outcome.tsx`). It calls `useTaskDone.complete` with `hold`, so the Done is immediate with its toast, Undo and `pagehide` beacon but its timer doesn't start, and opens the form with a fresh outcome id (`crypto.randomUUID()`). The form's text lives in a module-level draft keyed by task and outcome id, and the screens keep the task in the list (adding it back if a refresh drops it, for example after the beacon completed it), so the form survives refreshes, tab switches and view changes. Skip calls `release`, which starts the remaining Undo time. `Esc`, moving to another row or task, or switching or closing the panel call `leave`: with a note or next task typed it saves, otherwise it releases. Pressing Done on task B while task A's form has text saves A first and only starts B once that worked; a failed save shows a toast, keeps A done-held (not completed) and brings A and its note back (`onFailed`). The screen unmounting with text typed saves it and toasts what was saved (note, next task or both); if that fails the draft is kept as stranded, and the Tasks page (`restore`) reopens its form on the next visit. `beforeunload` asks before closing the tab. Saves run one at a time through a queue, so saving another task while one is in flight waits rather than failing, and a second Save on the same task returns the save already queued. Save calls `completeTaskWithOutcomeAction`, which returns `completed`; the client settles the held Done (`useTaskDone.settle`, which drops the entry and its Undo) only when the server says the task is completed, and if Undo was pressed while the save ran, `settle` reopens the task and says "Saved. Task reopened." instead of marking it done. When the save fails, or comes back with the task not completed, the form stays with the error so it can be retried (a not-completed reply also gets a fresh outcome id), and `useTaskDone.abandon` checks the task on the server and reopens it if Undo had been pressed. Undo also drops the form's draft. `completeTaskWithOutcome` (`lib/crm/task-act.ts`) locks the task and checks `canChangeTask`. An outcome id already in `crm.task_outcomes` returns that earlier result unchanged (and the task as it is now, so a stale replay after a reopen never reports it done). Otherwise it completes the task if it is open, then refuses when `crm.task_outcomes` already has a row for the task's current `completed_at` (a unique key, whoever saved it and whatever the links are now), creates the activity (`createActivityInDb`, `task_outcome` provenance, metadata `{ source: "task", taskId }`), creates the next task (`createTaskInDb`, the same links passed explicitly so nothing is detected, the task's assignee or the saver if the assignee has left, its ClickUp list) and records the outcome row with that `completed_at`. `crm.task_outcomes` (migration `20270120100000_task_outcomes.sql`) has row-level security through the task: anyone who can read the task can read its outcomes, and only someone who can change the task (assignee, creator, owner or admin) can insert one, as themselves. `applyTask` keeps a done task's `completed_at` when a restore writes another non-null time, so no second outcome window opens. Swipes and unlinked tasks keep the plain held Done. Rows show the discussion count; `/tasks?task=<id>` opens the panel directly, also for a task outside the current list. People who can change the task edit the title, assignee, due date, reminder and description there and mark it done with the circle by the title (`E` or `D`); tagged people get the same panel read only, with "You're tagged. You can read and discuss this task. Sam, Mark and admins can change it." Anyone can remove their own tag, and the assignee, creator and admins anyone's. On a shared doc the Discussion button (with its count) opens a panel in place of the backlinks rail, or a full-screen sheet on phones; private docs and daily notes have no button.

## Mail mirror

`lib/mail/` mirrors mailboxes so Orbit can show and search mail without asking the provider. `crm.mail_sources` has one row per mailbox (the main Microsoft connection, or an extra Microsoft account with `can_send`), with a provider (`microsoft` today, so Gmail can be added as another adapter), a lock (`locked_until`, five minutes) and the last error. `crm.mail_folders` holds each folder with its role (from Graph's well-known names: inbox, sentitems, drafts, archive, deleteditems, junkemail, outbox), counts, `sync_state` (`pending`, `backfill`, `live`, `skipped` for junk and outbox) and `sync_link`, the saved `@odata.nextLink` or `@odata.deltaLink`. `crm.mail_messages` holds every message: thread key (`conversationId`), subject, snippet, HTML body (up to 1.5 MB, else only text with `body_truncated`), plain text from `htmlToText`, sender, recipients, times, read, draft and flag state, attachments flag, categories, and a generated `search` tsvector (subject A, sender B, body C) with a GIN index. All three tables are owner-only with forced row-level security.

`syncMail` (`lib/mail/sync.ts`) loads the user's sources, claims each one, refreshes the folder tree every six hours (`listMicrosoftFolders`: the well-known ids, then up to five levels of child folders, at most 300), and splits the time between sources. Live folders run first (one delta round each), then pending and backfilling folders by role: inbox, sent, drafts, archive, others, deleted items. The first delta request selects the message fields with `$orderby=receivedDateTime desc`, `Prefer: odata.maxpagesize=50, outlook.body-content-type="html", IdType="ImmutableId"`. Each page is upserted in one statement through `jsonb_to_recordset`; partial items (only read or flag state) update those fields; `@removed` items delete the row from that folder only, so a message moved between folders keeps its row. A 410 or `SyncStateNotFound` resets the folder to `pending`. During a backfill, each page checks `pg_database_size` against `MAIL_STORAGE_LIMIT_MB` and pauses with a folder error when it is reached. Before any source runs, the database size is checked against `MAIL_HARD_STOP_MB` (default 18% above the limit); at or above it nothing syncs and every source gets `MAIL_HARD_STOP` as its error.

It runs from `/api/cron/mail` (`maxDuration` 300, a 240-second budget shared across users, with backfill), as the `mail_store` step of `/api/cron/sync` (15 seconds, new mail only) and after each CRM page load (10 seconds, new mail only). `lib/mail/store.ts` reads it: `searchMail` (`websearch_to_tsquery('english', …)` ranked by `ts_rank`, then newest; filters for sender, account and folder role), `getMailThread` (the thread in one source, oldest first, at most 100) and `mailSyncStatus` (stored against Graph's counts per folder).

### Mail on the timeline

`fileMailOnTimeline` (`lib/mail/timeline.ts`) runs at the end of every `syncMail`. As the user, it selects their mirrored messages from the last 60 days (not drafts, junk or outbox), pairs inbound mail with the sender and mail sent from one of their own addresses with each To and Cc address, and joins those addresses to workspace contacts by `normalized_email`. It skips any pair that already has an email activity on that contact with the same `mailMessageId` or `internetMessageId`, or, for activities logged at send time without those ids, the same title and direction within three minutes. For a contact it checks that contact's activities through the contact index. For mail filed on a company only, the JSON id lookups can't use an index under row-level security, so it reads the workspace's email activity ids once, and only when such rows exist. Up to 100 are filed per run, oldest first, as `email` activities with `source: microsoft`, `direction`, `account`, `mailMessageId` and `internetMessageId`. Filings older than a day carry `catchUp` in their provenance, and `enqueueWorkflowEvent` ignores events with `catchUp`. The old inbox sync (`lib/crm/inbox.ts`) skips a message whose `internetMessageId` is already on the contact. Every filer (this one, the old inbox sync and the send path) writes through `createEmailActivityOnce` (`lib/crm/activities.ts`), which takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock on the workspace and the message's `internetMessageId` (else its own id) and checks again before inserting, so filers that overlap write one activity and one `activity.created` audit event. A reply sent from Orbit is logged at send time with its `internetMessageId`, in the workspaces its conversation is filed in (`threadFiledWorkspaces` in `lib/mail/filing.ts`, the same rules as `effectiveFilingInDb`): nowhere when the conversation is ignored or only in Capture, and once in each workspace when it is filed in several. A contact newly cc'd into a reply on a thread routed elsewhere is logged where the thread is filed. New emails and forwards are logged in the current workspace. `sendContactEmail` (workflows, proposals and the contact page) sends with Graph's `sendMail`, which returns no message id, so its activity still relies on the title and time match.

### Mail home

`/mail` (`app/(crm)/mail/page.tsx`) renders the first page of the chosen view on the server and hands it to `components/mail/mail-screen.tsx`, which does everything else in the browser. `lib/mail/threads.ts` reads conversations: `listThreads` groups messages by source and thread key (`coalesce(thread_key, 'm:' || id)`) within a view (a folder role, one folder, or a search across every folder but junk and outbox) with window functions, takes the newest message for the row and `bool_or` for unread, flagged and attachments, and pages with a `(time, key)` cursor. `getThread` returns the whole conversation in every folder, oldest first, with HTML passed through `cleanEmailHtml` (`lib/mail/html.ts`: scripts, frames, forms, `base`, event handlers, `javascript:` links and `cid:` images removed) and `rich` set for designed emails. `mailNav` and `mailUnreadCount` count unread conversations per folder and account. The CRM layout doesn't wait for the count: `MailUnread` (`components/mail/mail-unread.tsx`) fetches `GET /api/mail/unread` (`mailUnreadCount`, which only classifies inbox threads with unread mail) after the page renders and again on focus at most once a minute, and Mail keeps it current through the companies store. `/mail` waits for the first page, the open conversation and the From accounts, and streams the nav to the browser as a promise (`use()` inside Suspense). Until it arrives the nav is `partial`: Mail shows the list, never the Connect Microsoft screen, and leaves the badge alone. If the nav fails on the server, the browser asks `/api/mail/nav` once. Client-safe types and URL parsing are in `lib/mail/view.ts`.

The browser talks to route handlers, not server actions, so reads are never queued behind writes: `GET /api/mail/threads`, `/api/mail/thread`, `/api/mail/nav`, `POST /api/mail/actions` and `POST /api/mail/sync` (an eight-second new-mail sync, returning the mail version). Migration `20270112100000_mail_speed.sql` adds `crm.mail_versions` (owner-only, one counter per user), bumped once per transaction by deferred triggers when something the list or nav shows changes: messages (folder, read, flag, subject, sender and the like), folder names and roles, mailboxes, reminders, scheduled drafts, sender rules, prepared replies, opens, and contacts' email addresses (for everyone in that workspace with mail). Sync bookkeeping (locks, delta links, counts, tokens) doesn't bump it, so a sync with no new mail leaves it alone. Changes it doesn't track show at the five-minute backstop or the next change: a mailbox's connected state (`mailbox_connections`, `calendar_accounts`), summaries, filing routes, subscriptions and capture dismissals. The poll every minute and on focus runs the sync and refetches the list and nav only when the version the sync returned differs from the one the last full refresh saw, or five minutes have passed. Reads, flags and moves update the nav counts in the browser (unread per account, inbox folder and split); after a move, Undo or failed read settles, one nav refetch runs 1.5 seconds later (later ones in that window ride along), so totals and the snoozed count stay right. Moving a sender to another split refetches the nav straight away. `/api/mail/thread` returns what the reader renders: plain text only when there is no HTML or for a draft, and the stored summary for the newest message as `cachedSummary`, or an empty one when the thread is too short or AI is off, so the browser skips `/api/mail/summary`. When a prefetched conversation arrives, the browser also fetches the attachment lists of the messages that open expanded (`loadAttachments` in `mail-cache.ts`, shared with `MessageAttachments`), so opening it never waits for Outlook. A conversation fetched before a local change or a forget is never stored over it. `components/mail/mail-cache.ts` keeps lists and conversations in memory for the session, collapses duplicate requests and prefetches two at a time: the first four of each list, the two after the selected conversation and the one before, and whatever the pointer rests on. Emails render in a sandboxed `iframe` (`allow-same-origin` and popups only, no scripts) with a content security policy that allows only images, styles and fonts, `<base target="_blank">`, and a height that follows the body.

`applyMailAction` (`lib/mail/actions.ts`) handles read, unread, flag, unflag, archive, trash, inbox and snooze for a conversation. Read touches every unread message and unflag every flagged one; unread and flag touch the newest message in the view. Moves take the messages in the current view (for a search, the inbox and your own folders for archive, everything but trash for trash, archive and trash for inbox), never drafts. It gets a token with `mailSourceToken`, calls Graph (`PATCH /me/messages/{id}` or `POST /me/messages/{id}/move` with a well-known destination, `IdType="ImmutableId"` so ids survive the move) four at a time, then updates the rows that succeeded, moving each to the folder Graph reports (or the folder with that role). The later delta for the old folder removes nothing, because the row is already in the new one. Changes to your own mailbox are not audit events, like notifications. The screen applies each change at once, undoes it if the request fails, and keeps moved conversations out of refreshed lists until the move is confirmed.

### Replies, drafts and read statuses

`lib/mail/compose.ts` writes new emails and replies as Outlook drafts. A new email (`mode` `new`) names its source account, starts with `POST /me/messages` and takes the draft's `conversationId` as its thread key; changing From on a saved one deletes it and starts again in the other mailbox. `components/mail/compose-dock.tsx` (`ComposerProvider` in `CrmChrome`, `useComposer().openComposer`) holds up to six floating cards, positioned along the bottom right until dragged, with minimised pills; Compose, `create-email` and Quick add's Send email open one, and `GET /api/mail/accounts` (`composeAccounts`: each mail source with its signature) fills From. `components/mail/mail-composer.tsx` is the editor shared by cards and inline replies. `saveMailDraft` checks the input (`readDraftInput`: mode, the message replied to, at most 100 addresses per field, 20,000 characters), and on the first save calls `createReply`, `createReplyAll` or `createForward` on that message with the source's token (`mailSourceToken`), which gives Outlook's subject and quoted history. It keeps the draft in `crm.mail_drafts` (owner-only: provider draft id, mode, the message, thread key, the quote, recipients, subject, body, tracking id). Every save PATCHes the draft with recipients, subject and `composeEmailHtml` (`lib/mail/html.ts`): the message as escaped paragraphs with links, the signature for the source's address, the read image, then the quote with any earlier read images removed, inserted after `<body>`. A draft deleted in Outlook is made again. `sendMailDraft` saves, adds attachments with `attachToDraft` (shared with large sends in `lib/microsoft/oauth.ts`), calls `send`, marks the tracking row sent, deletes the draft row and logs an outbound email activity on each matching contact in To and Cc. `discardMailDraft` deletes the Outlook draft and the mirrored copy. `getThread` returns the thread's drafts and leaves their mirrored copies out of the messages. The browser saves 1.5 seconds after typing stops, one save at a time, and sends any unsaved change with `keepalive` when the composer closes. Routes: `POST` and `DELETE /api/mail/drafts`, `POST /api/mail/drafts/send`, `DELETE /api/mail/draft-message?id=` (deletes a mirrored draft made outside Orbit, in Outlook and in the mirror), `GET /api/mail/people` (contacts and senders for the recipient fields).

Read statuses: when `crm.mail_preferences.read_statuses` is on (the default) and Orbit has a public `https` origin (`SITE_URL`, else the request), a draft gets a `crm.mail_tracking` row and its HTML a 1×1 image at `/api/mail/open/<id>`. That public route returns a GIF and calls `private.record_mail_open` (security definer, `crm_app` only), which records a row in `crm.mail_opens` unless the email is unsent or under five seconds old, the request comes from an address the owner used Orbit from in the last 30 days (`crm.mail_viewer_addresses`, an HMAC of the IP with `APP_SECRET`, noted after each CRM page load at most hourly), the last open was under two minutes ago, or there are already 200. The user agent is reduced to an app or device (`readerAgent`). The mail sync stores the tracking id on messages from the source's own address (`trackingIdIn`), and `listThreads` and `getThread` count and list the opens. `cleanEmailHtml` removes read images, so viewing mail in Orbit never counts as a read. Emails sent from a contact, a company or in bulk (`sendContactEmail`, also used by workflows and proposals) are sent as HTML through the same `composeEmailHtml` (with `plain`, so the text is escaped and linked but never formatted, and indentation, runs of spaces and blank lines are kept) and get the same tracking row and image. The row is marked sent after Graph accepts the email, deleted when Microsoft refuses it, and kept and marked sent when the outcome is unknown (a timeout); if the row can't be created the email goes untracked. It is linked to the timeline activity through `crm.mail_tracking.activity_id` in its own step after the activity is saved, so the shared activity carries no tracking id. Record pages read statuses with `timelineReads`, which reads `crm.mail_tracking` and `crm.mail_opens` inside `withActor`, so row-level security returns only the viewer's own.

### Remind me, Send later and Undo send

Undo send lives in the browser: `MailComposer` hands its content to `queueSend` in the compose dock and closes at once; the dock shows "Sent" with Undo and posts `/api/mail/drafts/send` after ten seconds, or straight away with `keepalive` on `pagehide`. The payload waits for any autosave in flight so a draft is never made twice. On the server, `writeDraft` reads the draft, its target and signature in one transaction and skips the Outlook PATCH when `content_hash` (subject, body, signature and recipients) hasn't changed since the last autosave, so a send is usually just Graph's `send`; a draft deleted in Outlook is made again and sent. A send by draft id first claims the row (`sending_at`, cleared on failure; the scheduler's `claimDueDrafts` sets it too), so Send now, the scheduler and a second click can't send the same draft twice: a row another send claimed in the last ten minutes is refused as already sending, and an id whose row has gone is refused as already sent or deleted. The composer sends a `sendKey` (a uuid made once per composer) and `crm.mail_send_keys` (owner-only) remembers it: a repeat of a key that already sent answers success without sending, a repeat while the first is still making its draft is refused as already sending, and a retry after a failure reuses the draft the first attempt made. Just before Graph's `send` (or SMTP for another address) the row gets `send_attempted_at` and `sent_internet_id`. A clear refusal (a 4xx, or a 500 with a specific Graph code, or an SMTP reply code) clears the mark; a lost answer (network error, timeout, 502, 503, 504) keeps it and answers "Outlook didn't confirm the send. Check Sent before resending." A later send of a marked draft first asks Outlook (`fetchDraftState` and `findSentCopy`). `findSentCopy` looks the email up by its `internetMessageId` when Orbit has one and trusts that answer alone; only without an id does it look in Sent Items for the same conversation and subject, sent at or after the attempt, to exactly the same recipients. A sent copy finishes the send without sending again ("Already sent."), and a Microsoft draft still in Drafts at least two minutes after the attempt is sent normally. Anything else, including every draft sent from another address (SMTP leaves the Outlook draft untouched, so its state proves nothing) and a draft that vanished when `send` answered 404, is refused with a 409 carrying `maybeSent` and the draft id: "May already be sent. Check Sent before using Send anyway." The composer that opens from that toast shows the message with Send anyway, which posts `force: true`; a forced send clears the mark and remakes the Outlook draft if it has gone. So Orbit never makes and sends a fresh draft on its own when the original's outcome is unknown; a draft already missing before the send is still made again. A failure that isn't a `CrmError` says "Outlook didn't confirm the send" only when the row was marked, otherwise "The email didn't send. Try again." Every send error carries the draft id, and the dock reopens the draft with that id and the same `sendKey`, so a retry reuses the server's draft. The browser hides a draft from the Mail cache (`holdQueuedDraft`) the moment its send is queued; Undo, a failure or any 409 releases it, which forgets and reloads every cached conversation it was hidden from. Attachments load alongside, and the timeline entry and attachment clean-up run in parallel after sending. `mailSourceToken` reads only the one source.

Snooze is the `snooze` mail action: `ensureMicrosoftFolder` finds or makes a top-level Snoozed folder (kept in `mail_sources.snooze_folder_id` and given the `snoozed` role on folder refresh), the conversation's inbox and own-folder messages move there, and a `crm.mail_reminders` row (`kind` `snooze`, owner-only) records when. Send later sets `mail_drafts.send_at` (with attachments, signature and `remind_hours`) through `POST /api/mail/drafts/schedule`; `DELETE` unschedules and `GET /api/mail/scheduled` lists them. A draft's files live only in Orbit, as `crm.email_attachments` ids in `mail_drafts.attachments` (the daily sweep skips any a draft still refers to), never in the Outlook draft: `writeDraft` carries them to the new row when changing From or remaking a lost draft, and each send attaches them once, just before `send`. `saveMailDraft`, `sendMailDraft` and `scheduleMailDraft` take `storedFiles`: the Mail compose routes pass `replace`, because the composer opens a draft with its files as removable chips (`MailDraft.attachments` from `threadDrafts` and `listScheduledDrafts`) and sends the whole list; the assistant, the MCP draft and send tools and the scheduled-send cron pass `keep`, which adds any new files to the stored ones. Sending with `remindHours` adds a `follow_up` reminder for the thread. Sending with `followUpDays` (1 to 10) instead adds one with `action` `send`, the written `follow_up_body` (or none), the `recipients`, the sending identity (`identity_id`, with `used_identity` so a deleted identity can't quietly change the From address) and `original_internet_id`, the `internetMessageId` of the email it was set on (read from the Outlook draft just before `send`, or the `Message-ID` of the MIME sent over SMTP), due at 09:00 London time that many working days later (`followUpDueAt` in `lib/mail/follow-up-rules.ts`, using the working days in `lib/crm/task-due.ts`), and closes any other waiting follow-up on the thread except a deferral. Drafts keep `remind_hours`, `follow_up_days` and `follow_up_body`, so a reopened or scheduled draft keeps the choice, and a scheduled one counts from when it actually sends.

`processDueMail` (`lib/mail/due.ts`) does nothing unless something is due. A reminder that throws is counted (`mail_reminders.attempts`, `last_error`) and tried again 5, 10, 15 and 20 minutes later; on the fifth failure it is surfaced as `due` with a notification ("Still snoozed" for a snooze) instead of retrying for ever. It claims due drafts (clearing `send_at` with `for update skip locked`) and sends them, recording `send_error` and a notification on failure; claims due reminders, moving snoozed conversations back to the inbox and marking the latest unread, and checking follow-ups for a newer message from anyone but the user's own addresses (done if there is one, otherwise a notification). A follow-up with `action` `send` (never an `automatic` one, which a check constraint forbids) goes through `sendDueFollowUp` (`lib/mail/follow-up.ts`): a reply marks it `replied`. It is only sent when the user's own message is still the latest in the thread, is the one it was set on (same `original_internet_id`, or, when either id is missing, sent no more than 5 minutes after the reminder was made), has the same To and Cc as the stored recipients, and the sending identity it was made with still exists. Otherwise it takes the written body or asks `writeNudge` with `unreviewed` (the thread and the user's voice samples only: no CRM context, call notes, edit examples or About you, since nobody reads it first; without the prepared replies setting or the daily limit) and stores the written body on the reminder before going further. It checks the mirror for a reply again, then asks Outlook directly (`fetchConversationMessages`: `GET /me/messages?$filter=conversationId eq '...'`) for any message in the conversation from an address that isn't the user's, newer than the reminder; a reply there marks it `replied`, and a failed check means it isn't sent. Only then does it move to `sending` (stamping `sending_at`) and send with `sendMailDraft` as a reply to that message, then `sent` with a "Follow-up sent to" notification. If any check stops it, or writing or sending fails, it stays `due`, which is the reminder, keeps any written text as a prepared nudge, and notifies with the reason. A row left in `sending` for over 10 minutes (the function died mid-send) is moved to `due` with the alert "Check Sent: follow-up may not have sent" and is never sent again automatically. `processDueMail` takes a `deadline` (20 seconds by default; `/api/mail/sync` passes what is left of its 30, the cron routes their own) and claims one draft or reminder at a time, stopping when less than 8 seconds (drafts) or 12 seconds (reminders) are left, so nothing is claimed that can't finish. `POST /api/mail/follow-up` saves, writes (rate limited), sends now (rate limited the same way when Orbit has to write it; a reply, a newer email, other recipients or a removed identity answer 409, a failed write, check or send 502), cancels (`cancelled`, also while the cron holds it as `due`, which stops the send; 409 "Already sending this follow-up." when it has moved past that) and restores one (only while its time is still to come and no other follow-up is waiting on the thread, else 409). The thread banner refreshes the thread after a 404 or 409. `getThread` returns the waiting follow-up with its body and recipients, and `followUpReplied` (who replied, and before which message) when the newest send follow-up on the thread was ended by a reply and no other follow-up is waiting; `listThreads` returns `followUpAt` while it waits. A surfaced reminder is `due`: `listThreads` sorts by the later of the newest message and `surfaced_at`, includes due follow-up threads in the inbox even when they hold only sent mail, and returns `reminder` and `snoozedUntil`. Archiving or trashing marks the thread's reminders done; unsnoozing ends its snooze. It runs from `/api/cron/mail-due` (`private.mail_due_members()` finds who has work), after each user in `/api/cron/mail`, in `/api/mail/sync` (Mail's minute poll) and after page loads.

### App and notifications

`app/manifest.ts` makes Orbit installable (standalone, `start_url` `/mail`, icons in `public/icons`), and `public/sw.js` shows pushed notifications and sets the app badge. Tapping one posts the link to an open Orbit window (iOS ignores `navigate()` on an existing window), or opens a new one; the worker also keeps the link for a minute so a window that starts up asks for it (`orbit-pending`). `PushRegistrar` opens it, and on Mail selects the conversation in place. `GET` and `POST /api/push/settings` read and save the mail level, meeting reminder minutes, first-read alerts and the Teammates switch; `POST /api/push/test` saves the device's subscription and sends it a test notification. The proxy lets `/sw.js` and the manifest through without signing in. `components/push/push-client.ts` registers the worker on every CRM page (`PushRegistrar`), subscribes with the VAPID public key from `GET /api/push/key`, and saves the subscription with `POST /api/push/subscribe` (owner-only `crm.push_subscriptions`); Mail sets the badge to the Important unread count. `isPushEndpoint` (`lib/push.ts`) only accepts `https` endpoints on the default port at FCM (`fcm.googleapis.com`, and `jmt17.google.com`, which current Chrome uses), Mozilla (`updates.push.services.mozilla.com`), Apple (`web.push.apple.com`, `*.push.apple.com`) and Windows (`*.notify.windows.com`), when subscribing and again before each send, so a member can't make the server call anything else. For anyone in more than one workspace, push, bell and booking email links go through `/open?workspace=&to=` (`alertLink` in `lib/crm/workspaces.ts`), so an alert from a workspace that isn't the current one switches to it first; `/open` only redirects to Orbit pages (`safeOpenTarget` in `lib/open-link.ts`), and the bell and `PushRegistrar` open a link for the current workspace in place.

`lib/push.ts` sends with `web-push`. The VAPID key pair is made on first use and kept in `private.push_keys` (the private key encrypted with `APP_SECRET`) through `private.push_key()`, so there is nothing to configure. Subscriptions the push service reports as gone (404 or 410) are deleted.

New mail: `storePage` returns the ids it inserted (`xmax = 0`), and for a live inbox those become `report.arrived`; after the sync `notifyNewMail` (`lib/mail/notify.ts`) keeps unread mail from others received in the last six hours, works out each one's split, applies the user's `notify_mail` level (`crm.mail_preferences`: important, all or none) and pushes one notification per message, or one summary for more than three. The first sync of a folder never notifies. For speed, each sync keeps a Graph change-notification subscription on the inbox (`created`, about three days, renewed within a day of expiry) pointing at `/api/microsoft/mail-webhook` with a random `clientState`; that public route echoes Graph's validation token, finds the owner through `private.mail_subscription_owner(id, clientState)` and runs a short new-mail sync after responding. Subscriptions need an https `SITE_URL`.

Other alerts go through `sendAlert` (`lib/alerts.ts`), which pushes, optionally adds a bell notification, and records a key in `crm.push_log` (owner-only, unique per user) so each alert goes out once. `calendarAlerts` (`lib/calendar-alerts.ts`) runs every minute from `/api/cron/mail-due` for the people `private.calendar_alert_members()` finds (a device set up and a meeting inside their lead time): one alert per meeting (keyed on iCal UID and start, so the same meeting in two calendars alerts once) whose link is the online meeting URL, which the service worker opens directly. `processDueMail` alerts on a failed scheduled send (always), an unanswered follow-up (with the bell) and a snooze coming back (both off when mail notifications are off). The tracking image route calls `alertFirstRead` after the first recorded open when `notify_reads` is on, using `private.mail_open_owner()`. `sendAlert` takes a `source` for its bell row (default `mail`).

Task reminders reach a locked phone through `processTaskReminders` (`lib/task-reminder-push.ts`), run every minute from `/api/cron/mail-due` with a 15 second budget. `private.claim_task_reminders(now, limit, skip)` picks open, timed tasks whose reminder time passed within the last 10 minutes and that were due no more than 5 minutes ago, locks them `for update skip locked` and stamps `crm.tasks.reminder_sent_at` in the same statement, so overlapping runs never send twice. It claims 10 at a time, looping while the budget lasts and skipping tasks this run already tried. Each row carries the recipient as a member row: the owner, or the creator when the task has no owner. Agent-made tasks with no owner, and recipients no longer in the workspace, get no push (the claim still marks them; the in-app poll still shows them). The migration marks every open reminder whose time had already passed as sent, so the first run after deploy doesn't send old ones. Each claimed reminder goes through `pushToUser` with the same title, body and tag as the in-app reminder (`reminderKey`), linking to the task panel; no bell row is written. A push that throws, or that reached no device and failed with a status other than 404 or 410, hands the claim back with `private.release_task_reminder`, so the next minute tries again while the window lasts. The `tasks_updated_reminder` trigger clears `reminder_sent_at` when `due_at`, `due_has_time`, `remind_minutes` or `owner_id` changes, and keeps `updated_at` unchanged when only the marker changes (it fires after `tasks_set_updated_at`, by name), so a claim never looks like an edit to ClickUp sync or the task list. In the browser, `TaskReminders` still shows its in-app alert, but skips its own system notification for a reminder the server pushes to you (`DueReminder.pushed`) when this device is subscribed, so you don't get two.

Teammate alerts go through `alertMembers(actor, recipients, alert)` (`lib/alerts.ts`), which callers schedule with `alertMembersAfterCommit` so it runs from `afterCommit` once the change is saved. It (and `alertMembersAfterCommit`, before queueing) refuses an alert without a source, title, key, tag or an Orbit link of up to 300 characters with no backslash or control character (and a workspace for `task` and `doc`). It follows the Meta leads pattern: recipients are resolved with `private.memberships_for` (one query for everyone, never the sender, and only people who share a workspace with the sender); then, six at a time, it acts as each recipient through `memberActor` and writes the `crm.push_log` key and the bell row in one transaction, so each alert lands once. Bell rows are always written, whatever the time. `chat` and `mail_share` alerts land in the recipient's home workspace; `task` and `doc` alerts land in the subject's workspace (`alert.workspaceId`), only for its members, because their links become a workspace switch through `alertLink` (a link that would pass 500 characters once wrapped is kept unwrapped). The bell (`listNotifications`, `markNotificationsRead`) shows `mail`, `chat` and `mail_share` rows in every workspace (`EVERY_WORKSPACE_SOURCES` in `lib/alert-sources.ts`) and other sources in their own workspace. Alert text is generic ("Sam mentioned you in #sales"), never message or email content, because bell rows outlive access. Once the rows are written, one batched Realtime call (`publishLiveBatch` in `lib/docs/live/broadcast.ts`, the `/realtime/v1/api/broadcast` endpoint with up to 100 messages a request) sends an `alert` event to each recipient's `docs-person:<id>` channel while, at the same time, each recipient whose Teammates switch is on (`notify_team` on `crm.mail_preferences`, read like `notify_mail`, default on) gets a push tagged per conversation or subject, so a burst replaces itself. The broadcast has an 8 second budget (logged when it runs out); pushes run alongside it and are awaited to their own 10 second timeout, so nothing is left running when `after()` settles. The result has `alerted`, `finished` (the broadcast settled in time) and `pushFailures`. The app badge has one definition, `badgeParts` (`lib/mail/notify.ts`): Important unread mail plus the unread teammate alerts the bell can clear in that workspace (`teamUnreadCount`). Pushes for new mail and teammate alerts carry it, `GET /api/notifications` returns it, and Mail adds the teammate count to its own Important count. Notification settings are one row per person: the `mail_preferences_personal_workspace` trigger puts every insert in the home workspace, and each setting is written with its own `coalesce`, so two changes at once both stick.

The bell is live. `AttentionProvider` (`components/crm/attention.tsx`) keeps the list and teammate count the layout rendered and refreshes both from `GET /api/notifications` (a route, so it never queues behind server actions) when an `alert` event arrives, when Realtime joins or joins again (catching up on anything sent before or during a drop), when the window regains focus (at most every 5 seconds), after the bell marks rows read (opening it clears every unread row in its scope up to the newest one it shows, `category is null`, so alerts beyond its ten rows stop counting in the badge, while one that arrives after it opened stays unread), and, when Realtime isn't joined or the server can't publish (local dev without a service key), every 60 seconds while the page is visible. Each refresh also sets the app badge. It listens through `watchLiveActivity` (`lib/docs/live/client.ts`) with no writing listener, which only joins the person channel; channels are shared per transport and topic, so the bell and Docs' activity watcher use one subscription, and a listener that throws never stops the others. The bell keeps its own read state, so a refresh never brings back a dot it already cleared.

### Split inbox

Splits are worked out when listing, not stored, so a new contact or rule changes them at once. The one part that comes from the mirror is kept as it changes: `crm.mail_sent_threads` (owner-only, select only) has a row per source and thread with a message in a sent folder (keyed by user, not workspace, so moving between workspaces can't hide it), maintained by triggers on `mail_messages` and on a folder's role under a transaction advisory lock on the source and thread, so the sent check is one key lookup. `mail_messages.thread_key` is never null: a trigger sets `m:<id>` when Outlook sends no conversation, so code can compare the column directly and use its indexes. `lib/mail/split.ts` builds the SQL: `splitJoin` finds the user's rule for the sender's address or `@domain` in `crm.mail_sender_rules` (owner-only; the address wins), and `splitCase` applies, in order, the rule, a calendar subject or sender, a matching contact (`normalized_email`), a sent message in the same thread, automated sender patterns (news), Outlook's `inferenceClassification` (`mail_messages.focused`, false means other) and finally important. The mirror reads `inferenceClassification` with every message; `classifyFolder` fills it in once for mail already stored, page by page, and sets `mail_folders.classified_at`. `listThreads` takes `{ role: "inbox", split }` (URL `view=inbox:news`) and returns each thread's split; `mailNav` returns totals and unread per source and split, and `mailUnreadCount` (the side menu) counts Important only. `POST /api/mail/sender-split` saves or clears a rule. Bulk actions in the browser move or mark each selected conversation with up to four requests at a time and one toast with Undo.

### Unsubscribe and subscriptions

Migration `20261222100000_mail_unsubscribe.sql` adds three owner-only tables with forced row-level security. `crm.mail_subscriptions` holds one row per user and sender key (an address or `@domain`, the same keys as `mail_sender_rules`): `status` (`subscribed`, `kept`, `unsubscribed`, `research`, `blocked`), `unsubscribed_at`, the last `method` (`one_click`, `mailto`, `page`, `none`) and `outcome` (`unsubscribed`, `requested`, `needs_you`, `failed`, `none`), `ignored_at` and `ignored_count`, `cold_pitch_at`, `pitch_rule` (Orbit made an Other sender rule for a cold pitch), and the last `List-Unsubscribe` header Orbit read with `one_click`. When an address row and a domain row both match, the stricter status wins (blocked, research, unsubscribed, kept, subscribed, the address breaking a tie), in SQL (`recordFor`, `effectiveRecord`) and on the Subscriptions page. `crm.mail_unsubscribes` records every attempt (sender, mailbox, message, method, outcome, the target host or address, the error, what happened to existing mail and how many emails moved, including ones moved in the background). `crm.mail_pitch_failures` records a failed cold pitch check or move per message. It also adds `mail_sources.research_folder_id`, `mail_preferences.cold_pitches` (`off`, `other`, `archive`, default `off`) and the `pitch` kind on `mail_prepared`. It can run twice. None of it is audited, like other changes to a person's own mailbox.

`lib/mail/unsubscribe.ts` parses the headers (`parseListUnsubscribe`: bracketed or bare URIs, folded lines, https and http pages, one-click only with an https link and `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click`). A `mailto:` keeps only its first address (from the path, else the first `to`) and its subject with control characters and line breaks collapsed, capped at 200 characters; `cc`, `bcc`, other recipients and `body` are ignored and the message is always "unsubscribe". `postOneClick` posts one-click: https on port 443 only, no credentials in the link, the host resolved and refused if any address is private, loopback, link-local, carrier-grade NAT, multicast or unique local (`privateAddress` from `lib/web-page.ts`) or the name is local or a metadata host; the request goes through `publicFetch`, whose resolver refuses the same addresses at connect time, with `redirect: "manual"`, each redirect re-checked (307 and 308 keep the POST, others become GET, at most three), a 10 second timeout and at most 64 KB of the answer read.

`lib/mail/subscriptions.ts` does the rest. `unsubscribePreview` and `unsubscribe` take the latest email from someone else in a thread (or from a sender), fetch its `internetMessageHeaders` from Graph (`fetchMicrosoftHeaders`, `GET /me/messages/{id}?$select=internetMessageHeaders`), refuse the user's own addresses and domains (`mail_sources`, `send_identities` and the sign-in address), refuse a protected sender with a 409 unless confirmed, then try one-click, then the first mailto (sent with `sendMailDraft` from the email's own mailbox, no signature, no read image and `logOnTimeline: false`), then the page (returned for the browser to open). A one-click link refused as private stops there: no mailto and no page. The preview returns the mailto address whenever one may be sent, so the dialog can say an unsubscribe email may go from the user's mailbox. `senderSummary` is the light preview for Block and Keep for research (no Graph call). Existing mail moves with `moveMessages` (`lib/mail/actions.ts`, the same Graph move and row update as the archive and trash actions, four at a time, reminders settled) across every mailbox: archive takes the inbox, trash takes the inbox, Archive, Snoozed and other folders. Moving runs for up to 25 seconds in the request, in pages of 2,000, and carries on with `after()` for up to 150 seconds (the routes allow 300); a move failure is returned as `moveError` beside the unsubscribe `detail`. `protectionFor` matches an address to a contact's `normalized_email`, a domain to any contact at that domain, and either to a company with that domain whose lifecycle is customer, client, prospect or partner. `POST /api/mail/unsubscribe` allows 30 a minute per person and `POST /api/mail/subscriptions/bulk` 3 a minute, through `private.consume_rate_limit`.

`listSubscriptions` groups inbound mail from the last 90 days (configurable) by sender, outside sent, drafts, junk and outbox and leaving out the user's own addresses and calendar senders, and keeps bulk senders: a stored unsubscribe header, a News rule, the automated sender patterns from `lib/mail/split.ts`, or "unsubscribe", "opt out" or "manage preferences" in the last 4,000 characters of the text. Senders with a status or a cold pitch are always listed. `suggest` (the bulk action) is a subscribed, unprotected sender with mail and no opens. Routes: `GET` and `POST /api/mail/unsubscribe` (preview, or with `mode=block` the light preview; unsubscribe), `GET` and `POST /api/mail/subscriptions` (list, keep, research, block, back to subscribed) and `POST /api/mail/subscriptions/bulk`.

`syncMail` passes the ids it inserted into a live inbox to `routeArrivedMail`, with its own deadline (at least 5 and at most 60 seconds), before notifying: mail from a blocked sender is archived, from a research sender moved to the Research folder (made on first use with `ensureMicrosoftFolder` and given a `mail_folders` row, role `other`), from an unsubscribed sender received more than 7 days after `unsubscribed_at` archived, unless the user has written in that conversation or ever emailed the sender, with `ignored_at` set only once the move succeeded, and from a cold-pitch sender archived when `cold_pitches` is `archive` and the user has never written to them. Routed mail isn't notified. `getThread` returns the sender's `subscription` for the reader's notice. `researchMail` reads research mail from the last hours for `search_email` and the morning brief.

Cold pitches piggyback on prepared replies: when `cold_pitches` isn't `off`, `replyCandidates` marks Important candidates whose sender isn't a contact, has no company with that domain, no subscription row or sender rule, and no other email from them in another thread or to them anywhere, and `prepareReply` asks for a `pitch` flag in the same call. A pitch is archived or given an Other sender rule first, then recorded as a `pitch` row with no draft. Candidates without a `pitch` row (prepared replies off, or past the run's six) are sorted in one batched call of up to 12, each cut to 1,200 characters. A failed call or move adds to `mail_pitch_failures`; that message waits 6 hours and is given up after 3 failures, as with email tone. Keep or back to subscribed deletes the Other rule a cold pitch made (`pitch_rule`).

### Attachments

`lib/mail/attachments.ts` lists a message's attachments from Graph (`/attachments`, inline images left out; `GET /api/mail/attachments?message=`) and streams one through `GET /api/mail/attachment` (`/attachments/{id}/$value`), served with a strict content security policy and `nosniff`; only PDFs, common images and plain text are shown inline, everything else downloads. Nothing is stored in Orbit.

### Shared threads

The owner of a thread can share it, read only, with people who share a workspace with them. Sharing is per thread: nothing else in the mailbox is exposed, only the owner adds or removes people (one at a time), and only the owner can reply or send. Mail actions are not audit events, so sharing records none.

**Tables** (migration `20270117150000_mail_shares.sql`, forced row-level security): `crm.mail_shares` (`owner_user_id`, `source_id`, `thread_key`, unique per owner, mailbox and thread; deleted with the mailbox) and `crm.mail_share_members` (`share_id`, `user_id`, `added_by`, `created_at`). `authenticated` can only select: the owner and members see the share's `id`, `owner_user_id` and `created_at` (not its mailbox or thread key) and every member row of their shares, through `private.my_mail_share_ids()`. No policy on `mail_messages`, `mail_folders` or `mail_sources` changes, and the owner's own Mail paths are untouched: shared rows never reach archive, Remind me, reply, prepared replies, summaries or read statuses.

**Definer functions** (granted only to `crm_app`, called with `query()`, never inside `withActor`): `private.mail_share_add(user, source, thread_key, people)` (the mailbox must be the caller's and the thread must have a message outside drafts, trash, junk and outbox; at most 50 people; everyone must share a workspace with the owner, checked under `for share` locks on their `workspace_members` rows; idempotent, returns the share, the newly added people and the others already in it), `private.mail_share_remove(user, share, member)` (owner only, "Only the person who shared this thread can remove people."), `private.mail_share_for_thread`, `private.mail_share_thread` (the owner's key back for a share), `private.mail_share_people(user, share)` (owner first, then members; names always show, an email only to the owner, the person themselves, or someone who shares a workspace with that person), `private.shared_threads(user)` (one row per thread shared with you: share, owner name, when you were added, subject, latest time, snippet, counterpart, message count, attachments; no bodies), `private.shared_thread(user, share)` (the newest 200 messages, oldest first, with only the reader's columns: no Bcc, `web_link`, provider ids, read or flag state, folder) and `private.shared_message_owner(user, share, message)` for attachments. Every read leaves out drafts and copies in drafts, trash, junk and outbox (`private.mail_share_messages`), and answers nothing once you are removed. A trigger on `crm.workspace_members` drops a member as soon as they and the owner no longer share a workspace, whichever of them left.

**Attachments** are visible to members. `openSharedAttachment` checks the message through `private.shared_message_owner`, then fetches that one attachment read only as the owner: `memberActor` from the owner's home membership and `mailSourceToken`, the token path background sync uses. If the owner's mailbox needs reconnecting the member sees "Couldn't open the attachment. Mark needs to reconnect their mailbox." with the owner's first name and no link to the owner's settings.

**Notes** are a Chat discussion of kind `mail_thread` (`chat_conversations.mail_share_id`, one per share through a partial unique index, deleted with the share), opened and read like task and doc discussions with the subject `{ kind: "mail_thread", id: <share id> }`. Its audience is the owner and the share's members (`private.chat_subject_open_to`, `private.chat_follow` and the select and insert policies). Tagging someone who isn't in the share saves nothing and gives no access: `postMessage` and `editMessage` refuse with a 409 whose details carry `needsShare: { people, canAdd }` (`canAdd` only for the owner, and only when every outsider shares a workspace with them), and `postMessageAction` and `editMessageAction` pass it on as `needsShare` beside `error`, so the UI can ask "Add Sam to this thread?". The owner's message reads "Sam isn't in this thread. Add them first."; a member's "Sam isn't in this thread. Only Mark can add people." The MCP and assistant Chat tools only reach rooms and DMs.

**Alerts** use source `mail_share`, land in the recipient's home workspace, carry no subject or body, and are never wrapped in a workspace switch (`alertMembers` and its push only wrap links for `task` and `doc`): "Mark shared an email with you" when added (key per share and add, linked to `/mail?shared=<share>`), "Mark mentioned you in a shared email" and "Sam replied in a thread on a shared email" (linked to `/mail?shared=<share>&discussion=1`). Owners get the same link; `GET /api/mail/shared/<id>` tells them `viewerIsOwner` with their own `threadKey` to open instead.

**API for the UI.** Types are in `lib/mail/share-types.ts` (no server imports), the service in `lib/mail/shares.ts`. Reads answer `{ error }` with 401, 404 or 502 through `mailResponse`.

| Call | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/mail/shared` | `{ threads: SharedThreadSummary[] }`, newest first, for the Shared tab. |
| `GET /api/mail/shared/<id>` | `SharedThread`: `owner`, `viewerIsOwner`, `threadKey` (owner only), `subject`, `people` (owner first) and `messages` (`SharedMessage`: cleaned `html` with `rich`, or `text`). 404 "Conversation not found." for anyone outside the share. |
| `GET /api/mail/shared/<id>/attachments?message=` | `{ attachments }` as `GET /api/mail/attachments`. |
| `GET /api/mail/shared/<id>/attachment?message=&id=&download=1` | The file, with the same headers and inline rules as `GET /api/mail/attachment`. |
| `GET /api/mail/share?thread=<key>&people=1` | `MailSharePeople`: `shareId` (null until shared) and `people`, one lookup when the thread isn't shared. The reader asks for this on every thread it opens (deduplicated, aborted when you move on). |
| `GET /api/mail/share?thread=<key>` | `MailShareControl` for the owner's share panel, loaded when it opens: `shareId`, `people` and `candidates` (people who share a workspace with the owner and aren't in it, with `role` in the owner's current workspace or null). |
| `shareThreadAction(threadKey, userIds)`, `removeFromShareAction(shareId, userId)` (`app/(crm)/mail/share-actions.ts`) | `{ ok, share, added }` or `{ ok, removed }`, else `{ error }`. |
| `GET /api/chat/discussions?mail_thread=<id>` (or `?share=<id>`), `openDiscussionAction({ kind: "mail_thread", id })`, then the Chat message actions and routes | The notes, as for task and doc discussions. |

**Live events** go to each person's `docs-person:<id>` channel as event `mail_share` with a `MailShareLiveEvent` payload; `watchMailShares(source, userId, onEvent, onJoined)` in `lib/docs/live/client.ts` listens on the shared person channel. `{ type: "shared", shareId }` to people just added; `{ type: "people", shareId }` to the owner and the other members when people are added or removed; `{ type: "removed", shareId }` to someone removed by the owner or by leaving a workspace (show "Mark removed you from this thread." and stop: the server already refuses them); `{ type: "message", shareId }` to members (sent in the background, never holding up Bin or Restore) when mail sync changes what the thread shows: a new message outside drafts, trash, junk and outbox, or a message that moves folder, stops being a draft, changes thread or is deleted (`storePage` reports the thread keys whose rows were inserted or changed, from the delta upsert and from moves and deletes found by `placeRemoved`). Sync checks once per mailbox run whether the owner has any share, and skips the lookup (`private.mail_shares_touched(user, source, thread_keys)`, one indexed query after each committed page that changed something, even past the sync deadline) when they have none. Moves made in Orbit (Archive, Trash, Move to Inbox and the like, through `recordMoves` in `lib/mail/actions.ts`) send the same event when a message enters or leaves what the thread shows (a move into or out of trash, junk, drafts or outbox), because the later delta sees no change; `crm.mail_versions` only covers the owner. Removal by `removeMember` sends `removed` and `people`; on any other path (a deleted profile or workspace) the trigger or cascade still removes access, no event is sent, and the UI refetches on focus and on a 404. Refetch on each. Notes use the usual `chat` events, whose `subject` is `{ kind: "mail_thread", id }`.

### Shared drafts

The server keeps the text of a shared draft, so the browser's copy can never overwrite a teammate's edit on any send path. The message is co-edited live (see Live body below); the subject still saves through `merge_shared_draft`, last write wins.

**Schema** (migration `20270118100000_shared_drafts.sql`, no change to `crm.mail_messages` or `crm.mail_drafts`): `crm.mail_shares` gains `draft_id` (references `crm.mail_drafts`, `on delete set null`, unique), `draft_state` (`sent` or `discarded` once the draft closes), `draft_held_until` (the owner's Undo window), `draft_version` (bumped by every text change and close) and `sent_internet_id`; `thread_key` may now be null, for a share that starts with a new email. A reply draft uses its thread's existing share (one shared draft per thread at a time); a new email gets a share of its own, which gains its `thread_key` when sent. `authenticated` may select `draft_id`, `draft_state`, `draft_held_until` and `draft_version` (never the mailbox or thread key), and the owner may update those plus `source_id`, `thread_key` and `sent_internet_id` on their own shares (policy `mail_shares_owner_update`, which also checks the draft and mailbox are theirs). That lets the owner's own transactions repoint and close a share next to the draft row they change.

**Lock order.** Every path that locks both takes the share first, then the draft: `merge_shared_draft`, `mail_share_draft_add`, `createDraft`'s repoint, Send's claim, schedule, unschedule, cancel, release and failure (`touchShare`, which also bumps `draft_version`), and the closes in `finishSend` and `discardMailDraft` (`closeShareInTx`). The background `claimDueDrafts` locks only drafts, and `storeDraft`'s final update locks only the draft.

**The share is the stable key.** `createDraft` with `previous` repoints the share to the new row, copies the previous row's subject and body into it, and deletes the old row in one transaction. That covers Outlook losing the draft on save or during Send, and From moving a shared new email to another mailbox: `storeDraft` makes the new draft first and only then deletes the old Outlook draft (`retireMovedDraft`), so a failure before the swap leaves the share on the old draft. If Outlook then refuses the change to the new draft, the error carries the new `draftId` and the composer adopts it, so its next save lands on the draft that holds the share. `finishSend` and `discardMailDraft` close the share in the same transaction as the delete (`draft_state`, `draft_version + 1`, the hold cleared, and for a send the Message-ID in `sent_internet_id`), then after commit `private.mail_share_draft_thread(user, share, thread_key)` (tried three times) moves a new email's share onto its thread for a Graph send. For an SMTP send, `private.mail_share_follow_sent_now` looks once for a Sent copy sync already imported (by Message-ID, in the last two days); otherwise, or if a step fails, `announceSharedThreads` calls `private.mail_shares_follow_sent` after each synced page (a failure there never stops the message announce) to set the thread once a non-draft copy with that Message-ID arrives, never onto a thread that already has a share and at most one share per thread. "Sent by Mark." stops showing once the sent copy is in the thread.

**Text.** Teammates and the owner's composer write through `private.merge_shared_draft(user, share, subject, body, base)`, which locks the share then the draft, refuses anyone outside the share ("You no longer have access to this draft."), refuses teammates while the draft is scheduled ("Scheduled. Read only until Mark unschedules it.") or its scheduled send failed ("Couldn't send. Read only until Mark unschedules it."), refuses everyone while sending (`sending_at` within 10 minutes or `send_attempted_at`) and teammates while the owner's hold is on ("Mark is sending. Read only."), refuses a teammate's change to a reply's subject ("Only Mark can change the subject of a reply."; the teammate editor shows it read only, as the owner's composer hides it), refuses a `base` older than `draft_version` (409 with `changed`), updates `subject`, `body` and `updated_at`, and never touches `content_hash`. `storeDraft` substitutes the row's subject and body for a shared draft before hashing and leaves them out of its final update (`case when` the row is shared), so a teammate's save during the Graph round trip is kept and the next save or send pushes it. `sendDraftOnce` reads the text inside `claimScheduledDraft` (the same `for update` that sets `sending_at`, after which merges are refused) and sends exactly that; scheduled sends build their input from the claimed row. Send and Send later check the email with the server's text for a shared draft. `refuseSharedTextChange` refuses a different subject or body from the assistant (`sendForAgent`, `change_scheduled_email`) and from MCP `mail_save_draft`; `mail_send` with `draft_id` already reads the row. Owner-only fields (From, To, Cc, Bcc, attachments, signature, Send later, follow-up) only ever come from the owner's own saves, and each such save tells teammates to refetch.

**Live body** (migration `20270119100000_live_drafts.sql`). The body reuses the Docs live client: `LiveSession` takes a `topic` (`draft:<share_id>`), its transport and its server by injection, `lib/docs/live/binding.ts` binds the Yjs text to the mail `BodyEditor` (which stops replacing its document from the `value` prop while live, and takes AI text through `setText`), and remote cursors come from `lib/docs/editor/presence` with every remote state shown as a person. `crm.mail_share_live (share_id, ydoc, generation)` holds the Yjs state, with forced row-level security and no grants: only definer functions touch it. `syncLiveDraft` (`lib/mail/live-draft.ts`, `GET` and `POST /api/mail/shared/<id>/live`) runs in one `crm_app` transaction (`appTransaction`, one pooled connection, no nested `query()`): `private.shared_draft_live_lock(user, share)` locks the share then the draft, checks access, and returns the body, the Yjs state, the generation, `draft_version` and the caller's refusal (`private.shared_draft_refusal`, which `merge_shared_draft` now calls too, so the two can't drift); the transaction sets `lock_timeout` to 3 seconds and a lock timeout is a retryable 503; Node merges the update, repairs the Yjs text to the row's body if something else changed it, and `private.shared_draft_live_store` writes the body (bumping `draft_version`, refusing again if locked or over 20,000 characters) and the state. So the stored body always equals the Yjs text, and every send path, which reads the row, sends the latest merged body. An update that changes anything while the caller is refused is a 409 with `locked`; the browser then syncs read only (no update) until a reply says it is open again, keeping its unsent typing unconfirmed (the reply is marked `held`), and while locked or read only it stops broadcasting its own updates to peers, so the owner's open session can't persist them. Removing a member sends `kick` on the draft topic; every open client treats it as a hint to sync, so the removed member's session gets a 404 and leaves the channel. A read only sync never bumps the version. Live body changes don't tell teammates to refetch; they arrive on the draft topic. An unreadable or oversized state is rebuilt from the body with `generation + 1` and a `pull` on the topic, and a stale client is told `rebuilt` and reattaches, rebasing its unsent typing. Trigger `mail_shares_live_reset` clears the state and bumps the generation whenever the share gains or loses its draft (sent, discarded, deleted, or a new reply draft), so an old client never merges into a new draft. Realtime policies `draft_live_receive` and `draft_live_send` let only the owner and the share's members join the topic; the dev relay checks `mail_share_open_to`. Send flushes the live state before the hold: the composer keeps the live session alive (`retain`) through its own unmount, syncs until the server holds everything it has, fetches the stored draft, refuses with "The draft changed. Check it, then send." if the body or subject differs from what the owner saw, then holds with the stored version and closes the session without saving again. A failed hand-over reopens the composer with the session's last confirmed text as its base, so nothing is typed twice. Autosave flushes before it writes Outlook. Teammates never touch Outlook or the owner's token.

**Hold.** `private.mail_share_draft_hold(user, draft, hold, version)` sets a two minute `draft_held_until` when Send is pressed, refusing if a teammate has saved since the version the owner's composer last saw ("The draft changed. Check it, then send."); Undo, a failed send (`releaseDraft`, `failDueDraft`) and the close clear it. Hold and release bump `draft_version`. Send later makes the same check: `scheduleMailDraft` takes `shareVersion` (the composer's `shareBase.version`) and refuses with the same message if it has moved. `changeScheduledDraft` sets `sending_at` on a shared draft for its whole Outlook round trip, so teammates stay read only until it is scheduled again; an unshared draft is left as it was. The version only moves when something changes: unschedule, cancel and Send's claim check the draft first and bump nothing when it has gone or is already sending. Because these bumps reach the composer only by event or refetch, it refetches after Unschedule and after Undo, refetches before Send later, and after a hold refused as changed it refetches and retries once when the text is the same.

**Reads** go through `private.shared_draft(user, share)`: owner, state (`editing`, `sending`, `scheduled`, `failed`, `sent`, `discarded` or `none`), mode, From, To, Cc (never Bcc), subject, body, the owner's signature for that address when the draft uses one, attachment names and sizes, `send_at`, version and save time. `private.shared_threads` also lists a share whose only content is a live draft. `GET /api/mail/shared/<id>` adds `draft` (`SharedDraftSummary`: state, subject, a 160 character snippet) to `SharedThread`. Every draft read in `lib/mail/compose.ts` (`DRAFT_COLUMNS`) carries `share_id`, so `MailDraft.shareId` is set wherever a draft is opened, Scheduled included.

**Calls.** `GET` and `PUT /api/mail/shared/<id>/draft` (`SharedDraft`; `{ subject, body, base }`, subject or body may be null); `GET /api/mail/drafts/share` (candidates) or `?share=<id>` (people); `POST /api/mail/drafts/share` `{ draftId, people }` (`private.mail_share_draft_add`, which `shareDraft` calls once more if a racing share inserted the row first, so the share lock is always taken before the draft lock: replies need the thread already shared and take no people, new emails need someone; alerts "Mark added you to a draft" with source `mail_share`); `POST /api/mail/drafts/hold` `{ draftId, sending, version }`. People are removed from a new email's share with `removeFromShareAction`. Every change sends `{ type: "draft", shareId }` on the owner's and members' `docs-person:<id>` channels, after commit.

**Editors.** Both editors keep a base (the text and version they last saw) and reconcile per field with `reconcileText` (`lib/mail/shared-text.ts`): a refetch takes the server's value wherever the local value still equals the base. They drop answers to requests older than one already applied and answers with a lower version, never move their base back after a save, refetch when clean on focus, on `visibilitychange` and when the live channel rejoins, and take the server's text when the draft turns read only. Unsaved text goes out with a `keepalive` PUT on `pagehide` and when the page is hidden, and on close after any save in flight. The owner's composer starts its base from the server, or from the base a handed-back draft carries (`shareBase`), never from a cached draft, merges the text before each save (retrying once after a 409, then "Couldn't save. Try again."), keeps no held copy in the tab and skips the closing `keepalive` save of owner-only fields. Send merges the text, then holds the draft; if either fails the hand-over fails at once, the Sent toast goes, nothing is sent, and the draft reopens with the error and its last known base. Read only states show a status line and a hidden caret, never opacity or height, and Cmd B and Cmd I do nothing. A change from the server is applied to the editor as the smallest edit (`minimalChange`), so the cursor stays put. Teammates edit in `SharedDraftEditor` inside the shared reader.

### Other sending addresses

`crm.send_identities` (owner-only) holds an SMTP identity tied to the mail source that receives its mail, with the password encrypted with `APP_SECRET`; `saveIdentity` (`lib/mail/identities.ts`) checks the SMTP login with nodemailer before saving (Neo's servers are preset); `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE /api/mail/identities` list, save and remove them. `composeAccounts` lists each identity as `smtp:<id>` with its source, and the composer sends `identityId` with the source's draft. A draft carries `identity_id`, and the signature and content hash use the identity's address. On send, `sendMailDraft` exports the finished Outlook draft as MIME (`/$value`), rewrites From and Date and drops Bcc, Sender and Exchange headers (`rewriteMime`), sends it over SMTP with every recipient in the envelope, then saves the same MIME to Sent Items (`POST /mailFolders/sentitems/messages`), deletes the draft and appends a copy to the provider's Sent folder over IMAP (imapflow). A send failure is stored as `last_error`. Messages flagged as drafts outside the Drafts folder are treated as sent copies.

### Prepared replies and automatic reminders
