`prepareMail` (`lib/mail/prepare.ts`) runs after each user's sync and due mail in `/api/cron/mail` and after a webhook sync that stored anything. It needs `LLM_API_KEY` and reads `mail_preferences.prepare_replies` and `auto_remind_days`. First it takes up to 12 sent messages from the last 48 hours that are the latest in their thread, have no waiting follow-up and haven't been checked, asks the model in one call which expect an answer, and records each in `crm.mail_prepared` (`kind` `follow_up`, `scheduled` or `skipped`, owner-only); a scheduled one adds a `mail_reminders` follow-up with `automatic` set, due after that many working days (`workingDaysLater`). Then, unless the day's 60 drafts are used, it takes up to 6 inbox messages from the last 48 hours in the Important split that are the latest in their thread, from someone else, with no draft, and asks the model whether a reply is needed and for the reply, with the thread, CRM context for the people, recent call and meeting notes, recent sent mail and the last edited drafts (prepared body against what was sent). The result is a `reply` row, `ready` with its body or `skipped`. A follow-up that comes due with no reply calls `prepareNudge`, which writes a `nudge` row for the user's latest message. `getThread` returns the ready draft for the latest message and any waiting follow-up; `listThreads` marks threads with one. `POST /api/mail/prepared` marks a draft `used` or `dismissed`, or cancels a reminder. On send, `notePreparedSent` moves a used draft to `sent` with the sent body (the edits it learns from) and anything else to `dismissed`. Every prompt treats email text as material to read, never as instructions, and nothing is sent automatically except a follow-up the user set to send.

Each reply and nudge is written for the workspace the thread is filed in (`threadWorkspace` in `lib/mail/filing.ts`, which `threadBrand` also uses). When it is filed in more than one, the home of the address it came to decides; when that still leaves it open, the cron's home actor is used and no brand note is added. The recipient context, call notes, writing notes, About you, brand note, booking link and the `mail_prepared` row (its `workspace_id` is no longer overwritten from the mail source) all come from that workspace. Voice samples are sent mail from addresses whose home is that workspace, sending addresses included, with signature blocks stripped, and edit examples are filtered by `mail_prepared.workspace_id`. The cold pitch check counts a sender in any of the user's workspaces as known. Write with AI resolves the thread's workspace the same way.

When the model marks a prepared reply as a meeting request it writes `[[times]]` where times go. `offerTimes` (`lib/mail/scheduling.ts`) takes the booking page rules for the reply's workspace (or the defaults, and no link, when that workspace has no page) with at least four hours' notice, reads the user's own busy calendar events through `withActor`, and `pickTimes` chooses up to three slots on different days, preferring 10:00 to 16:00 and moving from morning to afternoon. The body is rendered from the stored `template` and `slots`; `freshPrepared` re-checks them against the calendar when the thread is read and when a draft is used, and re-renders if any is taken or within an hour. `POST /api/mail/ai/event` (`eventFromThread`, `lib/mail/event.ts`) asks the model for a title, date, time, length and note from the thread and returns them with the conversation's other people (matched to contacts) and the invite setup; the reader opens the calendar's `InviteDialog` with that prefill, so sending goes through `createInvite` as usual.

Attachment text: `attachmentNotes` (`lib/mail/attachment-context.ts`) takes the last messages of a thread, and for any with attachments that haven't been read lists them through Graph, downloads up to four readable ones per message (15 MB cap, streamed) and extracts text with `readAttachmentText` (`lib/mail/attachment-text.ts`: unpdf for PDF, fflate plus the XML parts for docx, xlsx and pptx, and plain text and HTML). Each attachment gets a `crm.mail_attachment_texts` row (owner-only; `read`, `unreadable`, `too_large` or `failed`, text capped at 200,000 characters), so it is fetched once. The notes, labelled with file name, sender and date, are shared out within a character budget: 12,000 for prepared replies and Write with AI, 6,000 for summaries. Ask AI also searches read attachments with a full-text index and quotes `ts_headline` fragments.

### Not now replies

Migration `20261228200000_follow_up_signals.sql` adds `crm.mail_deferral_checks` (owner-only, forced row-level security: one row per inbound message, `none`, `found` or `failed` with a failure count) and four columns on `crm.mail_reminders`: `deferral` (true for a deferral's reminder, so it stays one when its email is moved or deleted and `deferred_message_id` is set null), `deferred_message_id`, `task_id` and `defer_note`. `prepareMail` looks for replies that put a conversation off until a named time ("Can we pick this up in January?", "circle back after Q1", "try me next year") in inbound mail from the last 48 hours whose sender is a live contact or has the domain of a company in Orbit (free-mail domains don't count). Only messages whose text names a later time (`deferralHint` in `lib/mail/deferral-dates.ts`; May and March only count after a word such as in, by, after, until, before, early, mid, late or of, or before a year, so "you may want" doesn't) reach the model, and the batch only looks at messages that are still the newest in their conversation. When prepared replies are on, the reply call for that message also returns `defer`; everything else goes in one batched call of up to 8 messages per run (`checkDeferrals` in `lib/mail/deferrals.ts`), so it works with prepared replies off. A failed call waits 6 hours and is given up after 3 failures. The model returns a precision (`day`, `week`, `month`, `quarter`, `year`) and the first day of the time named; `resolveDeferral` turns that into 09:00 London on a working day (task-due rules: weekends, bank holidays, the Christmas break): a day moves to the next working day, a week to its Monday, and a month, quarter or year to the first Monday of the month (on or after 8 January for January, since the first days back are busy). Anything under a day away or over a year away is ignored. A deferral adds a `follow_up` reminder (`automatic` and `deferral`, with the message, task and note; any other waiting follow-up on the thread ends, a send follow-up as `replied` since they answered and a plain reminder as `done`) and a task "Check in with <name>" for the contact and company, due at the same time with a reminder at the due time, whose description is the note and whose `mail_thread_key` pins the thread, which the task row shows as an email link. The task is created with `createTaskInDb`, so it records `task.created` with operation `defer_follow_up`. The note is cleaned (quotes, braces and brackets removed) and is given to the check-in prompt as material in the user message, not in the instructions. A thread gets one waiting deferral at a time: when a sync moves the email (deleting and reinserting it) and the new copy is found to defer again, the waiting reminder is pointed at the new message instead of adding a second reminder and task. The generic follow-up paths (`addFollowUp`, `cancelFollowUp`, which `POST /api/mail/prepared` uses, and a new send follow-up) never touch a deferral; only `cancelDeferral` ends one, so its task is archived with an audit event. `getThread` returns it as `deferral` (and leaves it out of `followUp`), and the reader shows "Follow-up draft on <day>" (with the time when it isn't 09:00 and the year when it isn't this year) under the deferring message, or under their latest message when that one has gone, with Change date (the Later picker with Next week, In two weeks, Next month and In three months, or a custom time) and Cancel. `POST /api/mail/deferral` moves the reminder and the open task together (`task.updated`), or ends the reminder and archives the task (`task.archived`). When the day comes, `processDueMail` handles it as any follow-up: done if they replied, otherwise `writeNudge` writes a check-in that picks the conversation back up, stored as a prepared nudge, and an alert. The check-in is drafted even when prepared replies are off (within the day's 60 drafts, and it counts toward them), because the person asked to be contacted then: the owner chose "draft and alert" for deferrals. Nothing is sent.

### Mail AI

`lib/mail/ai.ts` uses `completeJson` with the default model. Thread text comes from the mirror's plain text with quoted history cut (`stripQuoted`), newest messages first up to 16,000 characters. `summariseThread` stores one row per thread in `crm.mail_summaries` (owner-only) with the newest message id, so it is reused until new mail arrives; a single message under 280 characters isn't summarised. `draftWithAi` adds the people's CRM context and up to four of the user's own recent sent emails as examples of their voice, and asks for the body only. `askMail` first turns the question into up to four keyword searches with optional sender and date limits, runs them against the full-text index (falling back to no limits), then answers from the twelve best matches with numbered sources, which become thread links. Em and en dashes are replaced in everything the model writes. Routes: `GET /api/mail/summary`, `POST /api/mail/ai/draft`, `POST /api/mail/ai/ask`, each rate limited per user through `private.consume_rate_limit`. Semantic vector search is left out to keep the database within the Free plan.

## Relationship

`lib/crm/relationship-signals.ts` gathers signals per company or contact in two queries. The first reads email, meeting and call activities up to now, skipping titles that look like automatic replies or bounces and emails whose tone row says `automatic`. It splits them into inbound (`direction = inbound`, or old inbox mail with a `from`), outbound and meetings, with counts, latest dates, the outbound emails since they last replied or met, and engagements in the last 90 days. The second finds the latest tone in the last 120 days from `crm.activity_tones` or a call's `sentiment` (healthy, watch and at risk map to positive, neutral and negative). `scoreRelationship` (`lib/crm/relationship-score.ts`) turns them into a label and a reason. `listCompanies` adds `relationship` when asked with `relationships: true` (the Companies page, `/api/v1/companies`, the assistant and MCP), `getCompanyContext` always does, and the contact page and Tasks call `relationshipsFor`.

`crm.activity_tones` holds one row per inbound email activity: `tone`, `automatic`, a short `reason` and the model. Members read and write their workspace's rows; agents only read. `classifyEmailTones` (`lib/crm/email-tone.ts`) runs at the end of `syncMail` when ten seconds or more remain, sending up to 12 unclassified inbound emails from the last 120 days to `completeJson` in one request.

## Boards

A board is a row in `crm.pipelines` with `kind = 'board'` (the default pipeline is `kind = 'sales'`), its own `crm.pipeline_stages`, a `position` for the side menu, and `add_on_won`. Cards are `crm.opportunities` rows on that pipeline: one active card per company per board (checked in `insertOpportunity`), named after the company, with `expected_close_date` used as the due date. Board stages have no won or lost flags and need at least one stage; sales pipelines keep exactly one won and one lost stage. Archiving a board archives its cards.

Lifecycles are free text (1 to 40 characters). `lead`, `prospect`, `customer` and `churned` are built in: `cleanLifecycle` (`lib/companies.ts`) maps their keys and labels to the key, and `matchLifecycle` in `lib/crm/companies.ts` gives any other name the spelling a company in the workspace already uses. `lib/crm/lifecycles.ts` lists them with counts, and renames or deletes custom ones by rewriting each company through `setCompanyLifecycleInDb` (one `company.updated` audit event each; delete moves them to `lead`). Built-in ones can't be renamed or deleted.

When an opportunity on a sales pipeline becomes won (on create or update), `afterWon` sets the company's lifecycle to `customer` with an audit event and adds a card to the first stage of every active board with `add_on_won` that doesn't already hold the company.

Adding is inline. `components/crm/record-combobox.tsx` is a search box with a listbox in a popover anchored to the input; it searches through `searchRecords` in `record-picker.tsx` (contacts scoped to a company with `searchCompanyContactsAction`, or to people with any company when the company is still empty) and offers "Add “…”" (a new company) or "Add … at <company>", which call `addCompanyInlineAction` and `addContactInlineAction`. Enter before results arrive waits for them and picks the exact match, else the first. `opportunity-composer.tsx` uses two of them as a card in a stage (focused without scrolling the page) or as a row under the list, and saves with `addOpportunityInlineAction`; `inline-adders.tsx` holds the Contacts and Companies rows. Opening an existing card or opportunity still uses the sheet.

## Proposals

| Table | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `crm.products` | Name, description, `price_gbp`, `price_usd` (at least one), `billing` (`one_off`, `monthly`, `yearly`), `archived_at`. |
| `crm.document_templates` | `kind` (`proposal`, `nda`), name, `status` (`draft`, `active`, `archived`), `blocks` and `settings` (accent colour, valid days, and `logo`, a PNG data URL of up to 300,000 characters made in the browser) as JSON, checked by the zod schemas in `lib/proposals/model.ts`. |
| `crm.documents` | One proposal or NDA: template, title, `status` (`draft`, `sent`, `viewed`, `client_signed`, `completed`, `declined`, `withdrawn`), company, contact, optional opportunity, owner, currency, `items` and `blocks` copied from the template and products, `valid_until`, `current_version_id`, view counts and the time of each step. |
| `crm.document_versions` | What was sent: `content` (the filled-in blocks, items, totals, both parties and the consent text) and `content_hash`, a SHA-256 of its stable JSON. |
| `crm.document_links` | The client's link for a version: `token_hash` (SHA-256), `token_cipher` (encrypted with `APP_SECRET` so Orbit can show the link again), `revoked_at`. |
| `crm.document_signatures` | One `client` and one `sender` signature per version: name, title, company, typed signature, consent text, method (`email_link` or `orbit_login`), IP address, browser and time. |
| `crm.document_events` | Created, sent, viewed, client signed, countersigned, completed, declined, changed and withdrawn, with who, IP address, browser and data. `occurred_at` defaults to `clock_timestamp()` so events in one transaction keep their order. |
| `crm.document_files` | The `issued` PDF (rendered on send) and the `executed` PDF (rendered on completion), with their SHA-256. |

All of them are shared workspace records with `_member` and `_agent` policies like the rest of the CRM. Drafts can be deleted; sent documents can't. Versions, links, signatures, events and files are insert-only (links may only set `revoked_at`), so what was signed can't be changed after the fact.

`lib/proposals/catalogue.ts` holds products and templates, and `lib/proposals/documents.ts` the rest. `createDocument` checks that the template is active and the contact works at the company, prices the items in the chosen currency, and links the company's only open sales deal. `sendDocument` refuses missing fields and square-bracket placeholders, snapshots the content into a version, renders the PDF (`lib/proposals/pdf.ts`, pdf-lib with the standard fonts and a signing certificate page), creates a 24-byte link, sets the status and emails it through `sendContactEmail`. `reviseDocument` revokes the link and returns to draft. `countersignDocument` needs `client_signed` and the owner, a workspace owner or an admin, then stores the executed PDF and emails the client.

The client page (`app/p/[token]`) and its routes (`/api/p/[token]/sign`, `/decline`, `/pdf`) are public in `proxy.ts`. `private.document_link_owner` (granted only to `crm_app`) turns the hashed token into the document, version and owner; `lib/proposals/public.ts` then acts as the owner through `withActor`, like booking pages. It counts views (not for signed-in members), records `document.viewed` the first time, and alerts the owner in the bell and by push, deduplicated per hour. `clientSign` needs `agree: true`, signs the current version only, and records `document.signed`; signing is limited to 20 attempts per IP address in 10 minutes.

Audit events `document.created`, `.sent`, `.viewed`, `.signed`, `.completed`, `.declined`, `.withdrawn` and `.changed` feed webhooks and the `proposal_event` workflow trigger, whose context adds `proposal` (title, kind, status, total, currency, valid until, decline reason, who signed, Orbit and client links) with the company and contact. `proposalsForDeals` gives Opportunities the latest proposal per deal, by deal or else by the company's unlinked proposal.

## Workflows

`crm.workflows` belongs to its owner (`owner_id`) and stores `definition` (`{version: 2, trigger, triggerPosition, nodes, edges}`, validated by `lib/workflows/definition.ts`: every edge leaves a real output of its source (`out`, or `true`/`false` for If, `each`/`done` for For each item), each output connects to at most one node, and the graph has no cycles), `trigger_type` for matching, `status` (`draft`, `active`, `paused`), `is_template`, and `next_run_at` for schedules. Members read their own workflows and shared templates; owners and admins read all.

Events: `recordAudit` calls `enqueueWorkflowEvent`, which maps the audit event to trigger types (`eventTriggerTypes`) and inserts into `crm.workflow_events` inside the same transaction, carrying the source workflow id and depth from the provenance. `private.dispatch_workflow_events` turns each event into `crm.workflow_runs` for every active workflow with a matching trigger type, skipping the workflow that caused the event and anything three levels deep, snapshotting the definition, then deletes the event. Finer trigger filters (field, stage, board, activity type, "only mine") are checked when the run starts; a run that doesn't match is deleted.

Runs: `private.claim_workflow_runs` claims queued runs, waits that are due, and runs stuck for ten minutes, with `for update skip locked`, only for owners still in the workspace. `executeRun` (`lib/workflows/engine.ts`) builds the context as the owner (`buildContext`: company, contact, deal, activity, call summary and transcript, task, change; for `task_completed`, `task` also carries its notes without the Mail link lines, its links, its opportunity's name and `openTasks`, up to 10 other open tasks on the same person, company or opportunity assigned to the owner (or unassigned and created by them); with `mine`, a completion with no person behind it (an agent) only matches when the task is the owner's in that same sense; a Create task step with link `task` copies the completed task's person, company and opportunity, and fails with a message on any other trigger), then follows the graph from the trigger's `out` edge, storing the current node in `state.at` and a stack of loop frames, and logs each node to `crm.workflow_run_steps`. When a path ends (no edge from the chosen output, or Continue only if is false), the innermost loop moves to its next item or, when done, carries on from its `done` output; with no loop open the run finishes. Older definitions saved as nested step lists are converted to a graph when read (`safeDefinition`), and runs started under the old format are cancelled with a message. Waits store `resume_at`; approvals store the pending step in `state` and notify the owner, and `decideApproval` resumes or cancels the run, only as the owner's actor for the run's own workspace (a run in another workspace is refused with a message to switch to it there). Runs and their steps are private to their owner: the policies on `crm.workflow_runs` and `crm.workflow_run_steps` show rows only to the run's owner, admins included, so an admin looking at a colleague's workflow sees no runs, no last run status and a run count of 0. Owners and admins see that a colleague's approval is waiting through `private.waiting_approvals_of_others(user, workspace)`, which returns only the run id, workflow name, approval title, owner name and time. Every write a step makes goes through the normal domain functions as the owner, with provenance `source: workflow`. A run stops starting steps eight seconds before its deadline and is re-queued. Test runs execute AI and logic steps and replace actions with a description of what they would do. Post to Chat (`chatStep`) finds the room among the owner's own rooms (`findMyRooms`, by id, or by name for definitions written by the assistant; when more than one room has that name, the one linked to the run's workspace, otherwise the step fails asking to pick it). Saving a definition that changes a step's `roomName` but keeps its `roomId` clears `roomId` (`settleChatRooms`), and people other than the owner see Post to Chat steps without their room (`mapWorkflow`), so a shared template never shows a private room's name. Only member tags typed in the template alert; tags that come from the run's fields are turned into plain text (`chatBody`). It posts through `postMessage` with `viaWorkflowId`, so mentions alert as in Chat. Its `client_key` is `wf:<run>:<step>` plus `<loop id>.<index>` for each open loop (hashed when over 100 characters, `chatPostKey`), so a run reclaimed after ten minutes finds the message it already posted instead of posting again. A room that is gone or that the owner has left fails the step ("Couldn't post to Chat: you're no longer in #sales."), which alerts the owner like any failed step. `private.claim_due_schedules` leases due schedule workflows; the engine queues a run and sets the next time in London time.

Capabilities beyond the basic nodes, all in `lib/workflows/engine.ts` unless named:

- Ask AI can set `webSearch`, which sends `web_search_options` through `completeJson` (`lib/llm.ts`, which also returns the gateway's URL citations) and adds an optional `sources` list output merged with those citations, and `context`, which appends the run's company and contact, their last 10 activities, open and recent deals, and the owner's last 8 emails with the contact (`recordContext`). The system prompt tells the model that emails, pages and records are material, not instructions.
- `find_records` (`lib/workflows/records.ts`) selects up to 100 companies or contacts through `withActor` with lifecycle, tag, owner, open deal, days since the last activity and search filters, ordered by `last_contacted_at` (latest activity), creation or name. `skipDays` leaves out records the same workflow found within that window, using `crm.workflow_record_marks` (owner-only, written only on real runs). Each record item carries `type` and `id`; when For each item reaches one, `applyLoopRecord` loads it as the run's company and contact for that iteration, and the outer company and contact come back when the loop ends.
- `upsert_record` finds a company by domain or normalised name, or a contact by email, and creates it unless `create` is off (companies through `upsertCompany`, which schedules enrichment). `useInRun` makes it the run's record.
- `webhook` posts a rendered body (JSON when it parses, otherwise text), or the run's data as JSON, to an https address, refusing private and local hosts and redirects, with a 10 second timeout.
- Schedules can be monthly (`dayOfMonth` 1 to 28).
- `email_received`: after a mail sync stores new inbox mail, `queueMailWorkflowEvents` (`lib/workflows/mail-events.ts`) checks the owner has an active email workflow, classifies each message (bounce by sender or subject, with the failed address taken from the body; automatic reply by subject; otherwise any, marked as a reply when the user sent earlier in the thread) and inserts a `crm.workflow_events` row with `owner_user_id`. The dispatcher only matches events with an owner to that owner's workflows, and the insert policy only lets a person set themselves as owner. `buildContext` loads the message as the owner and the contact by sender, or by the bounced address. The Graph webhook runs a workflow tick when events were queued, and `/api/cron/mail` runs one every time, with the last 40 seconds of its budget kept for it.

`runWorkflowTick` dispatches events, starts schedules and executes claimed runs until its budget, less an 8 second reserve, runs out. It runs in `after()` on CRM page loads, at the end of every `/api/cron/mail`, from `/api/cron/workflows`, and straight after an event is queued. `/api/cron/sync` has no time left for runs, so it calls `queueWorkflowWork`, which only dispatches events and starts schedules. A workflow's Send email step passes the workflow's provenance (workflow id and depth) to the email activity, so the loop guard sees workflow-sent email. Its `from` config holds a sending address from `listSenders` (empty means the main mailbox); the engine matches it by address at run time and fails the step when that address can no longer send. A workflow shown to someone other than its owner, or copied from a template, has `from` cleared along with its Chat room.

`crm.notifications` holds in-app notifications per user (approvals, failures, Notify someone steps), readable and updatable only by that user; any member may insert one for another member of the same workspace. `category` tags a notification for something Capture shows (`capture`: approvals, No reply yet and Time to check in alerts, calls with no next step); the bell lists only untagged ones (see Capture).

## Linear

`lib/linear.ts` calls Linear's GraphQL API with `LINEAR_API_KEY` (sent raw in `Authorization`), scoped to the team whose key is `LINEAR_TEAM_KEY` (default `CNT`). The team, its workflow states and labels are cached for five minutes. Searches filter by team key and either issue number (for `CNT-123`) or title and description, excluding completed and cancelled issues unless asked. The assistant's create, comment and update tools are write tools, so they need approval.

## Email templates

| Table | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `crm.proof_entries` | Client proof for cold emails, shared by the workspace: `client` (1 to 120 characters), `description` (up to 300, required when described only), `product` (free text up to 80 characters, empty when not given; migration `20270103100000_personal_memory_workspaces.sql` dropped the old Eclipse or Cntent check and turned `eclipse` and `cntent` into `Eclipse` and `Cntent`), `used_for`, `result` (1 to 500), `measured`, `permission` (`named`, `described` or `reference`), `approved_by` and `review_on` (kept, no longer shown or used), `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_at` and `archived_at`. Workspace members can select, insert (only as themselves in `created_by`) and update the content columns; removing archives. Agents and anonymous users have no access. |
| `crm.email_templates` | Reusable emails shared by the workspace: `name` (1 to 120 characters, unique per workspace on `lower(name)` among rows with no `archived_at`), `subject` (up to 300), `body` (plain text, up to 20,000, line breaks kept), `tags` (up to 20, normalised like contact tags, GIN-indexed), `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_at` (set by `private.set_updated_at`), and `archived_at`. Workspace members can select, insert (only as themselves in `created_by`), update the content columns, and delete; agents and anonymous users have no access. |

`lib/crm/email-templates.ts` validates and normalises writes, lists templates newest first with search over name, subject, and body and a tag filter, duplicates as "Copy of …" with a free name, archives or deletes, and counts tags. Personalisation reuses `lib/crm/personalise.ts`; `renderTemplate` fills the tokens for one recipient. The settings pages live under `app/(crm)/settings/` with a shared layout (see Settings).

## Quiet relationships

A company is quiet when its latest activity is older than 21 days (`QUIET_DAYS`), or it has never had one. Company context also reports a relationship status: `new`, `active`, `quiet`, or `needs_action`. `listCompanies` with `quiet: true` (`quiet=true` in the URL and `GET /api/v1/companies`) adds `not exists` a live activity in the last 21 days on the company or on one of its live contacts, so it combines with every other filter, sort, paging and export. It backs Activity, Gone quiet in the Filter menu on Companies (a `choices` row on `PresenceFilter`); `/quiet` redirects to `/?quiet=true&sort=strength&order=asc`. `findStaleRelationships` still serves the assistant, MCP and `/api/v1/relationships/stale`.

## Capture

Capture is everything waiting on the person in the current workspace, in five sections in this order: Approvals, Follow-ups, Replies, Tasks and To file. Each person sees only their own items; owners and admins also see a read-only Waiting on others list. The page (`app/(crm)/capture/page.tsx`, `components/capture/`) shows chips with each section's count (empty sections hidden, All always shown, keys `1` to `6` through the `tab-N` targets), the sections, and "Nothing waiting" when everything is empty.

`captureContext` (`lib/crm/capture.ts`) is worked out once per request and per person, and shared by the layout's badge (`captureStatuses`) and the page: the person's workspaces (`workspaceActors`), then, in parallel, the `unknownMail` scan and `waitingOnce` (`lib/crm/action-needed.ts`), which reads the person's own items for all their workspaces and tags each with the workspace it belongs to:

- Approvals: `crm.workflow_runs` owned by the person with `status = 'waiting'` and `waiting_for = 'approval'`. Approve and Reject go through `decideApproval` as the person's actor for the run's workspace (Capture lists approvals from every workspace), the same path as the Workflows page (the decision recorded in the run state, the approval notification marked read, the run continued with `executeRun`). `listWaitingOnOthers` returns, for owners and admins only, other members' waiting approvals in this workspace that have waited more than one working day (`pastOneWorkingDay`: past the same time on the next working day, counting from the next working day when it started on a weekend or holiday), with their owner and the working days waited. The run select policy already lets admins read them; Capture only links to the workflow and never decides for someone else.
- Follow-ups: `crm.mail_prepared` rows with `kind = 'nudge'` and `state = 'ready'` whose email is still the newest in its conversation (nothing later outside drafts, outbox, junk and trash), with that email's opens, plus due follow-up reminders (`crm.mail_reminders` with `kind = 'follow_up'` and `state = 'due'`, one per conversation) that have no ready nudge and nothing newer in the conversation since they surfaced (or from someone else since they were made), shown as No reply yet or, for a deferral, Time to check in, plus the workspace's `crm.follow_up_suggestions` (calls with no next step, the latest 50, counted with the same limit). Review and send on a nudge opens `/mail?thread=…&compose=prepared`, which starts the reply with the follow-up exactly as Mail's Edit and send does (`compose=reply` starts an empty reply). Dismiss calls `updatePrepared(…, "dismiss")`. A reminder row's Reply opens the conversation with a reply started and Done sets the reminder to `done` (`finishReminder`).
- Replies (`lib/mail/waiting.ts`): the person's inbox messages from the last 30 days that arrived before the start of the second working day back (`workingDaysCutoff`, London time, using `nonWorkingReason` from `lib/crm/task-due.ts`), from an address that isn't theirs (their mailboxes, send identities, profile email and `profiles.other_emails`), with one of their addresses in To (not only Cc), not automated or a calendar sender, in the Important split (`splitCase`), and with no later message in the conversation outside drafts, outbox, junk and trash (and none from their own addresses anywhere). Conversations ignored in Capture, newsletters, blocked or cold pitch senders (`mail_subscriptions`), ready `pitch` rows and bodies with an unsubscribe footer are left out, as is anything in `crm.capture_dismissals` at or after the newest message. At most 100, longest waiting first; a conversation that is also in To file shows there only. `waitingDays` counts working days after the day it arrived up to today; the tag is amber from 3. Done is Mail's archive (`applyMailAction` from the inbox), Snooze is Mail's snooze with its Later today, Tomorrow and Next week times, and Dismiss upserts the dismissal with the message time, so a newer email from them brings it back. The candidates come from `mail_messages_folder_received_idx` and each later-message check from `mail_messages_thread_idx`; the unsubscribe check runs last, on rows in order, until 100 pass. Thread checks compare `thread_key` directly (a conversation keyed `m:<id>` has only that message, so `sameThread` in `lib/mail/split.ts` looks it up by id), never `coalesce(thread_key, …)`, which can't use the index. On a 60,000-message mailbox the whole `captureContext` takes about 175 ms under row-level security.
- Tasks: the person's own tasks and unowned ones (like `mineFilter` in `lib/crm/tasks.ts`; row-level security still limits a member to the ones they made) that are open, not archived and due before the end of today, overdue first, at most 100 per workspace. Done is `completeTask`; Snooze (`snoozeTask`) moves the due time through `updateTask` (audited with operation `snooze_task`) to one of `taskSnoozeChoices` (`lib/crm/task-snooze.ts`, worked out in the browser when the menu opens): the next working day, or the first working day of next week, keeping the task's time of day (09:00 when it has none). A time picked on a weekend, bank holiday or over Christmas moves to the same time on the next working day (`onWorkingDay`), in the browser for the toast and again on the server.

Mail follows the person, so nudges, reminders and replies are placed by `placeThreads`: with one workspace everything is in it; otherwise the first of the person's workspaces (oldest membership first) that the conversation is filed in, by its Filed in choice, then a domain rule for the other person's domain, then contacts and companies matched by `routedSql`, else the home workspace.

Each item has an arrival time for the dot: an approval when it started waiting, a nudge when it was written, a reminder when it surfaced, a reply the start of the day it reached two working days (`replyArrival`), a task the later of its creation and the start of its due day. `captureStatus` adds the items for its workspace to the To file and call follow-up counts, so the badge, `fresh` and `newestAt` cover every section and the switcher dot for another workspace includes them. Someone in one workspace never has other workspaces counted. A section that fails to read (each runs in its own savepoint) is listed in `failed` on the context and the status: the page shows "Replies couldn't be read. Reload to try again." in that section, and the side menu keeps the last count it had for it instead of dropping to nothing.

Clearing is one row at a time. The row goes and the counts drop at once (the page puts what it has hidden in the store as `captureCleared`, which the side menu and the bell subtract), and a toast offers Undo for six seconds. Only then, or when another row is cleared, the page is left or the tab is hidden (with `keepalive`), does the browser post the change to `POST /api/capture` (`readCaptureAction` and `runCaptureAction` in `lib/crm/capture-actions.ts`), so Undo just cancels it. The post carries the workspace the page was showing as `captureWorkspaceId` (kept apart from a Move to's own `workspaceId`, which it used to overwrite, so a move went nowhere), and `captureActor` runs the change as that workspace's actor, refusing with 409 when the person is no longer in it, so a change still pending during a workspace switch lands in the right place. A change that fails puts the row back and says why ("That didn't save. Check your connection and try again." when the network failed); after a contact is made or a conversation filed, a toast names the contact with Open. A tapped row collapses over 220 ms and further clears are ignored for 250 ms; the highlighted row moves to the next one (or the previous one at the end). `captureCleared` is left in place when the page closes and reset when the side menu next gets counts from the server after the change (`captureLive` stops that while Capture is open, where the page works it out from what it hid). Coming back to the tab refreshes Capture. The route revalidates the layout and, in `after()`, summarises a filed import, relinks the calendar after a contact is made and pushes a finished or moved task to ClickUp.

The bell (`components/companies/header/notifications/notifications.tsx`) no longer lists tasks, and `listNotifications` leaves out notifications tagged `category = 'capture'` when they were made (`notify` and `sendAlert`'s `bell.category`: workflow approvals, "No reply yet" and "Time to check in" alerts, calls with no next step), so it never matches on title text. They are still stored and still pushed, and Capture always lists what they are about. It starts with "N waiting in Capture" and the count per section, and its red dot means unread updates only. The Workflows item no longer shows an approvals count.

Keys (`lib/shortcuts.ts`, Capture group, as row keys on the highlighted row): `A` approve or add, `R` reply or reject, `E` Done or Dismiss, `H` snooze, `F` file, `I` dismiss, Enter opens, `Z` undoes and `1` to `6` pick a chip. A row shows its keys only while it is highlighted from the keyboard. Row keys whose buttons sit in More on a narrow screen still work: `F` and other plain actions through hidden key targets, `H` by opening More (the shortcut provider takes the first usable `data-row-key` in the row). On a phone (`components/capture/capture-row.tsx`, with Mail's `watchSwipe`), swiping left clears and right snoozes; approvals don't swipe, and More holds the actions that don't fit. A swipe commits by the row's width (`width` in `SwipeHandlers`), so it commits whenever it looks armed; Mail still uses the window width.

Migration `20261230100000_action_needed.sql` adds `crm.capture_dismissals` (owner-only, forced row-level security, one row per person, kind and conversation, put in the home workspace by `private.personal_row_workspace()` and moved by `private.move_personal_rows`), partial indexes for waiting approvals, ready prepared drafts and due follow-up reminders, an index on `contacts.normalized_email` for the Important split, and `crm.notifications.category` (checked to `capture`), backfilling older approval, No reply yet, Time to check in and `/capture` alerts. It can run twice.

To file: `listToFile` (`lib/crm/capture.ts`) returns the person's own unfiled imports in this workspace (Move to sends one to another workspace's Capture, see Calls across workspaces) (`listUnfiledImports` with `mine`, so `conversation_imports.user_id` is them; Granola and Teams imports always carry the importing user) and, when the user has a Microsoft mailbox row, people met (`listPeopleMet`). Each item has an arrival time: an import's `unfiled_at` (set by the `private.stamp_unfiled_at` trigger on `crm.conversation_imports` when a row is inserted as, or moves to, `unfiled`; older rows were backfilled from `updated_at`), or a person's `firstSeenAt`, the earliest `starts_at` of their meetings (stable when a calendar is reconnected and its events are saved again; a person only counts as arrived once that meeting has started). `crm.capture_seen` holds one `seen_at` per user and workspace (owner-only, forced row-level security; select, insert and update for `authenticated`; existing members were seeded with the migration time). `captureStatus` counts To file in SQL rather than building the lists: `count(*)` and `count(*) filter (where arrived > seen_at)` over the person's own unfiled imports (no 50 row cap), and the same count over people met, worked out in SQL the way `listPeopleMet` does it (attendees and organiser of the user's events on calendars used for CRM, not cancelled, a valid address, not a workspace member's or the user's own address or domain unless free-mail, not a live contact and not hidden). It returns `waiting` (every section), `sections` (the count per section), `fresh` (arrived after `seen_at`, or everything when there is no row), `newestAt` and `seenAt`. To file says "Showing the latest N of M" when its lists are shorter; the CRM layout passes it to the sidebar, which puts it in the Zustand store so the section header's menu and expand buttons can show the dot too. Opening Capture calls `markCaptureSeen` in `after()` with the request time (`greatest`, so it never moves back), and the page sets the store's `captureSeen` to the newest arrival it showed, so the dot clears on client navigation without a reload. Follow up: `crm.follow_up_suggestions` (owner-only, forced row-level security, unique per user and call activity) holds calls that ended with no next step. `followUpCalls` (`lib/crm/follow-ups.ts`, through `followUpCallsAcross` for each of their workspaces) runs for each person in `/api/cron/mail` with a 30 second deadline and at most 2 drafts a run, and as the `call_follow_ups` step of `/api/cron/sync` (after Granola and summaries, at least 25 seconds left, the same 30 second cap) for people with a workspace or personal Granola key and no Microsoft mailbox, so Granola-only people get them too. A draft that fails is marked `failed` and retried after an hour, up to 3 attempts. It takes that person's filed Teams and Granola imports (`conversation_imports.user_id`, the mailbox owner or Granola note owner, since the call and its mail are theirs) with a ready summary, from the last 14 days and at least 2 working days old (the call's time of day on the second working day, or 09:00 out of hours), for a company with an open deal (status `open`, stage neither won nor lost). A call is skipped when, since it ended (`ended_at`, or an hour after it started), the person has emailed anyone at the company (mirror sent mail to a contact's address or the company domain, or an outbound email activity they logged; a free-mail company domain such as gmail.com only matches its contacts' own addresses), any meeting or call with the company has been logged or is in their calendar; or when any task for the company or its people has been created since the call started, open or done (the owner's rule: a task means someone has a next step); or the summary has an accepted action item. Tasks are read through row-level security, so a member can't see a colleague's private task and may still get a suggestion for that call. The draft goes to the call's contact (or the first participant at the company domain, never at a free-mail domain): a recap and one concrete next step in the user's voice (recent sent mail and About you), with a short label for the call ("Demo", "Intro call"). The meeting title, names, summary, key points and action items come from invites and transcripts, so they go in the user message as material and the instructions only refer to them. The row is flagged once, with one alert (`sendAlert`, key `no-next-step:<activity>`, linking to `/capture`; push follows the mail notification level; the bell leaves it out because it waits in Capture). Each run marks ready rows `done` once a next step appears or the deal closes. Capture lists ready rows under Follow-ups: Review and send opens the compose dock with the person, subject and draft (`openComposer` takes `subject`, `body` and `onSent`) and marks the row `used` only once that email is sent (after the undo window) or scheduled, so closing the composer leaves it in Capture; Dismiss marks it `dismissed` once its Undo has gone. `listFollowUps` and `closeFollowUp` need `crm:read` like the rest of Capture. `captureStatus` counts ready rows in `waiting`, `fresh` and `newestAt`, under `sections.followUps`. `connectionAlert` turns the mailbox, calendar, Teams calls, Teams transcripts and Granola statuses into one line (or `null`) for the Capture alert. The connection cards, Sync now actions (`app/(crm)/capture/actions.ts`, which redirect to Settings › Email or Settings › Connections with `error` or `info`) and the Microsoft OAuth return for the main mailbox (`/settings/email?connected=1` or `?error=`) live on Settings › Email and Settings › Connections.

### Capture relevance

Email from new people (`unknownMail` in `lib/mail/filing.ts`, the scan above) only reaches To file when the sender might do business with the workspace the email belongs to. `lib/mail/capture-filter.ts` wraps the scan and `lib/mail/relevance.ts` holds the logic. Each candidate already has its workspace from `captureWorkspaceSql` (the thread's Move to, a domain rule, the receiving address's home, else the mailbox's workspace), and every decision, cache entry and rule is for that workspace.

`judgeInDb` decides each candidate without a model, in this order:

1. A rule in `crm.mail_sender_relevance` (`rule = true`) for the exact sender in that workspace, then one for `@domain` (free-mail senders only have exact rules). Rules come from Never ask (`muted`), Show in Capture (`business`, exact sender) and filing (Create contact & file or File to records `business` for the sender, and for their domain only when it isn't free mail or on the list and has no rule yet, so filing never replaces a Never ask rule; the address rule wins for that address only).
2. Someone the user has sent mail to (To, Cc or Bcc, from the sent folder) or met (a past, uncancelled calendar event with at most 25 attendees listing them) is shown (engine `history`). This beats every decision below, so a sender hidden earlier comes back once the user writes to or meets them. The facts come from `crm.mail_known_addresses` (one row per user and address with the sent count, first and last sent and first met), kept by `private.mail_known_sent` on `crm.mail_messages` (a sent message counts once, when it is inserted; a move into Sent from another folder marks the address written to without counting again, and a re-sync that leaves the folder unchanged does nothing) and `private.calendar_known_met` on `crm.calendar_events` (only when an event is inserted or its attendees or organiser change), both inserting in address order so they can't deadlock each other, and backfilled by the migration, so a page load looks up only the senders being judged.
3. `listedSender` (`lib/mail/senders.ts`): `LISTED_SENDERS` by category, matched on the sender's domain and its parent domains, plus `SENDING_DOMAINS` (pure sending domains such as `facebookmail.com`). The whole domain is hidden only for the collections list and the sending domains. At other listed domains only strictly automated local parts (`AUTOMATED_LOCAL`: noreply, notifications, statements, mailer-daemon, bounce and the like, matched as the whole local part) and sending subdomains (`email.`, `mail.`, `news.` and the like, not `comms.`) are hidden. Every other address there, people and role mailboxes such as accounts@, enquiries@ and info@ alike, goes to the classifier with the listed category as a hint (`email.senderDomainIs`), so public sector, NHS and bank senders are never labelled Personal by the list alone. The list is skipped for a domain the user has sent mail to or that has activity with a contact in that workspace. Free-mail domains are never listed.
4. The thread's own decision in `crm.mail_thread_relevance`, used only when it was made for the same workspace and the same sender, about the thread's latest message (`message_id`; rows without one fall back to the decision time). A failed decision (`category` null, engine `failed`) shows the email and is retried after 30 minutes.
5. The sender's cached business decision from Jev or Haiku in `crm.mail_sender_relevance` for that workspace, if under 60 days old (copied to the thread as `cached`). Only business is cached: a decision to hide is never reused, so each of a sender's other emails is judged on its own subject and content.
6. With neither `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` nor `LLM_API_KEY` set, the email shows. Otherwise it is pending: out of sight and counted ("Sorting N" under To file) until it is sorted or its newest message is five minutes old, when it shows. Pending and skipped keys are kept out of Replies.

Outlook's Other split and automated, newsletter, cold pitch and unsubscribe mail never get this far (the scan's own filters). A Jev or Haiku decision hides an email only when it isn't business and its confidence is 0.8 or more; a missing confidence shows it. List, header, rule and history decisions have no confidence and always apply.

`classifyItems` sorts pending candidates, four at a time, grouped by workspace and run as that workspace's actor:

1. `historyInDb` runs one query per batch for the user's own mail: messages from the sender and their domain (through `crm.mail_messages.from_domain`, a plain column kept by `private.mail_message_from_domain` and indexed with `user_id`, because an expression index can't be used under row-level security), mail to them and people met from `crm.mail_known_addresses`, first and last dates, and activities with contacts at that address or domain in that workspace. Only counts and dates leave the database, never other threads' text.
2. Someone the user has written to is `business` (engine `history`) with no model call.
3. In the background only, the message's headers from Graph (`fetchMicrosoftHeaders`, one lookup of provider ids per run): `Precedence: bulk|list|junk` or `List-Id` is newsletter, `Auto-Submitted` other than `no` is service (engine `headers`). Header decisions are stored on the thread only, never cached for the sender. Sync doesn't store headers, because the delta query can't return them.
4. Jev (`lib/jev.ts`, shared with the cold email check) when `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is set: one `choice` question `category` whose criteria are the seven categories, and one `noul` question `could_do_business`, whose probability becomes the confidence (`1 - p` for a category other than business). Jev gets at most half the time left when Haiku can follow. If Jev isn't set, fails, times out or gives no category, Haiku 5.5: `completeJson` with model `claude-haiku-5-5`, `CLASSIFIER_PROMPT` and a zod schema of `category`, `confidence` and `reason`, with the deadline when at least 8 seconds remain and otherwise one attempt within the time left. If both fail, or anything else throws, the thread is stored as failed and shown.
5. Within a run a sender's decision is shared with their other threads only when it shows the email; when it hides, each other thread is asked about on its own.

What leaves Orbit for each decision (`decisionState`), to TypeSafe at `api.typesafe.ai` (Jev) or, as the fallback, the AI gateway (`LLM_API_BASE_URL`, default `api.llmapi.ai`), and nothing else:

- `business`: the workspace's name, What you sell, Ideal customer and Former names from that workspace's brand profile.
- `person`: the user's name, and their role and About you for that workspace.
- `history`: the counts and dates above, as short phrases, plus `userHasWrittenToSender`, `userHasWrittenToDomain`, CRM activity at the domain and meetings.
- `email`: sender name and address, the receiving address, subject, snippet, a signature (the last eight lines of the email's own text before any quoted reply, at most 600 characters, read from the start of the body) and, for a listed domain, what kind of organisation it is.

Nothing from another workspace is included; the mail and meeting counts are the user's own, which follow the person. About you and the business context are sent on purpose. Personal mail can be sent before it is known to be personal, except where the list already hid it.

When it runs: `classifyCapture` after mail sync in the CRM layout's `after()` (20 seconds, skipped when a run started in the last 60 seconds), in `/api/cron/mail` for each person (30 seconds), after a Microsoft webhook sync that stored mail (10 seconds) and after the Capture page when something was still pending (25 seconds), each at most 25 candidates a run. One run per person at a time: `crm.mail_relevance_runs` (owner-only, forced row-level security) holds a claim that lasts two minutes or until the run ends, and a run releases only its own claim. `captureContext(actor, now, { inline: true })` (the Capture page and `get_capture`) sorts up to 6 pending candidates on the spot and waits at most 2.5 seconds; anything unfinished carries on in `after()`, and a background run in the same process skips threads already being sorted.

Personal and Collections mail is never suggested and never learned from. Sensitive means confidently Personal or Collections for that workspace: a thread decision in that workspace from the list or headers, or from Jev or Haiku at 0.8 or more, with no business rule for that exact sender there (filing and Show in Capture set one, and also mark that sender's threads business). Exclusion is per thread: a sender's other threads are kept unless the user has an explicit Personal or Collections rule for that sender. `learnPersonal` leaves out sent prepared replies whose thread is sensitive, whose people include a sender with such a rule in that workspace (`noSensitivePeopleSql`), or whose people include an automated personal or collections address on the list (`sensitiveAddress`). Public sector and bank people, low confidence calls and decisions made in another workspace are kept. `learnBusiness` leaves out tones of email activities from sensitive threads, or from senders with such a rule, for every member of the workspace, through `private.sensitive_email_activities(workspace, since)` (security definer, `crm_app` only, called with `query()`, same rules). Memories already learned before a thread was marked can't be traced back to it (their sources are labels, not threads), so they are not removed. Mail itself is untouched.

Capture shows "N not shown" under the sections (for All and To file): the skipped emails in this workspace with their label (`SKIPPED_LABELS`: Supplier, Service, Personal, Collections, Spam, Newsletter, Never ask) and reason, Show in Capture (`email-show`), and the workspace's rules (Never ask, Always show from Show in Capture, and domain rules from filing) with Hold to delete (`rule-remove`, `removeSenderRuleInDb`). Never ask is a row action (in More on a phone) with its own Undo toast, and Dismiss's toast also offers it (`email-never`, which dismisses the thread and saves the `muted` rule in the Capture workspace). `get_capture` returns `toFile.notShown` with the count, the count of Personal and Collections email (`personalOrCollections`) and the other emails with their category; mail that is Personal or Collections in its own right (its category, or the thread's stored decision, for example one later covered by Never ask or shown at low confidence) is only counted, in the To file emails too (`personalOrCollectionsEmails`). Shown business mail from public sector, health and bank domains is listed. `search_email` and `get_email_thread` still read any thread when the user asks, because it is their own mail. The Not shown list shows the mailbox owner the subject of Personal and Collections mail so they can check nothing important was hidden.

People you've met (`listPeopleMet` and its count in `captureStatus`) leaves out `isMeetingNoise` addresses: booking and meeting tools (Calendly, Zoom, SavvyCal, Cal.com, Chili Piper, Acuity, Doodle, YouCanBook.me, HubSpot meetings), Google Groups, Google and iCloud calendar resources, room and group mailboxes (`room@`, `boardroom@`, `conference-room@` and the like) and automated senders.

`askJev` and `completeJson` refuse to call the network under the Node test runner unless given a `fetchImpl` or a replaced global `fetch`, and `npm test` blanks both keys.

Migration `20270112700000_capture_relevance.sql` runs with `lock_timeout = '3s'`. It adds `crm.mail_sender_relevance` (unique per user, workspace and sender), `crm.mail_thread_relevance` (unique per user, source and thread), `crm.mail_relevance_runs` and `crm.mail_known_addresses`, all owner-only with forced row-level security and grants to `authenticated` only, partial indexes for personal and collections senders, the nullable `crm.mail_messages.from_domain` column with its trigger (no rewrite; existing rows are filled in batches by hand on the hosted project) and the `(user_id, from_domain)` index (created only when missing, so build it with `create index concurrently` first), an `activity_tones (workspace_id, created_at)` index, `private.sensitive_email_activities`, and `private.move_personal_rows` again (the `20270108100000_settings_simplified` version plus deleting a leaving member's relevance rows for that workspace and, when they exist, moving `crm.calendar_event_notes` and `crm.calendar_event_links`, so it ends the same as `20270113000000_calendar_notes.sql` whichever runs last). It adds the column and the triggers first, refuses to run as a role that can't bypass row-level security, and can run twice. It builds test and local databases; the hosted project is migrated from separate staged scripts instead (tables and functions, then the column and triggers, then the `mail_known_addresses` backfill one user at a time, then the `from_domain` backfill in page ranges, then the indexes concurrently), because the file's backfill would hold write locks on `mail_messages` and `calendar_events` for its whole run. Deploy the code only after both backfills have finished: until then nobody counts as written to or met, and older senders show 0 received mail.

## What is left for you

The hosted project is Orbit (`xuvofoydyewbloybrmas`) in `eu-west-1`. The app connects through the session pooler as `crm_app`, and webhook delivery uses `crm_admin`. `withActor` sends `begin`, the JWT claims and `set local role` in one round trip, and schedules webhook delivery (in `after()`) only when the transaction queued a delivery; the cron also retries due deliveries. Do not run `supabase/bootstrap/local-auth.sql` there. The Microsoft inbox starts once the Entra app credentials are in the environment. Automatic transcripts need a Granola key (`GRANOLA_API_KEY` or a per-person key in Settings), `CallTranscripts.Read.All`, `SITE_URL`, `CRON_SECRET`, and the Supabase `pg_cron` job in the README. ClickUp needs `CLICKUP_API_TOKEN`, the migration `20261018100000_clickup.sql`, and the lists chosen in Settings. Gmail is still separate. Apply `20261022100000_multi_calendar.sql` before deploying the Calendar page, `20261023100000_calendar_feeds.sql` before deploying calendar links, `20261025100000_email_templates.sql` before deploying email templates, and `20261026100000_booking_pages.sql` before deploying the booking page (the same applies to every later migration, recorded in `private.schema_migrations`); extra calendar accounts from other tenants need Orbit's Entra app to allow other organisations (and personal accounts if wanted).

## Docs

Docs (the UI name; tables are `pages` because `crm.documents` holds proposals) are Markdown notes. Markdown in `content` is the source of truth; everything else is derived from it on every save.

| Table | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `crm.page_folders` | Owner (`user_id`), `parent_id`, name, `position` among siblings, `shared`, `effective_shared`, `archived_at`. |
| `crm.pages` | Owner, `folder_id`, `kind` (`note` or `daily`, with `daily_date`), title, `content` (up to 1,000,000 characters), `word_count`, `revision`, `shared`, `effective_shared`, `assistant_can_edit`, `ydoc` (the live Yjs copy, see Live editing), a generated `search` tsvector (title A, the first 200,000 characters of content B), who edited last (`edited_by` `person`, `assistant` or `agent`, with user and agent ids), `versioned_at`, `archived_at`. One daily note per person per date. |
| `crm.page_links` | One row per distinct `[[link]]` or `@` mention in a page: `target_kind` (`page` or a record kind), `target_key` (the lowercased title), `target_page_id` (null while unresolved), `target_record_id`, `label`, `heading`. |
| `crm.page_tags` | One row per inline tag on a page, lowercased, nested with `/`. |
| `crm.page_versions` | Snapshots: title, content, reason (`created`, `edited`, `renamed`, `before_agent`, `restored`, `link_renamed`), editor, user, agent and a summary. |

**Privacy.** Every page and folder has an owner and is private to them. A page is visible to every member when `effective_shared` is true, which is its own `shared` flag or its folder's `effective_shared`. Triggers keep it right in the same transaction: `private.pages_place` sets it on insert and whenever `shared` or `folder_id` changes, `private.page_folders_place` does the same for folders (and refuses a folder inside itself), and `private.page_folders_spread` walks the subtree when a folder's `effective_shared` changes and updates every folder and page below it. Policies then only compare columns: members see `effective_shared or user_id = session user` in their workspace, and `crm_agent` sees `effective_shared` notes only, so agent tokens never see private docs or daily notes. Links, tags and versions follow their page through an `exists` on `crm.pages`. Members can't update `effective_shared` or `user_id` (column grants), and agents can't change `assistant_can_edit`. Only the owner can make a page or folder private (`lib/docs`), because the row would stop being visible to anyone else. Daily notes can't be shared or put in folders (a check constraint). A page or folder can only go into a folder that is shared or has the same owner, and a move that shares something or makes it private is refused with `visibility_change` unless the caller passes `allowVisibilityChange`. A page left in a folder its owner can no longer see (for example after the folder's owner unshares it) lists under no folder.

**Save path.** `commitPage` in `lib/docs/store.ts` is the one place content changes: create, edits from the editor, the assistant, MCP and REST, renames, restores and daily appends. It checks `baseRevision` (409 on a stale save), parses links, tags and mentions with `lib/docs/markdown.ts` (code blocks and inline code don't count), resolves each `[[Title]]` (or `[[Title#Heading]]`, `[[Title|label]]`) to a page by case-insensitive title among pages the source page's owner can see, preferring their own, then shared, then the most recently edited, and keeps unresolved links. `private.pages_resolve_links` resolves waiting links when a page with that title appears, is renamed, shared or restored, and unresolves links whose source owner can no longer see the target. A rename rewrites `[[Old]]` to `[[New]]` in every linking page the renamer can see, through the same path with a `link_renamed` version; a page they can't see keeps its link by id and gets its text fixed the next time it is saved.

**People tags.** A `member` token survives a save only when the page tags people (`tagsPeople`: a `note` with `effective_shared`) and the person is a member of the page's workspace. `keepMemberTags` runs in `commitPage` and `createDoc` before links are planned, so every path (editor, live sync, MCP, REST, the assistant, paste, daily appends, restores) turns other member tokens into plain `@Name` and no chip or `page_links` row is left behind. Before `writeIndex` replaces the links, `newlyTagged` reads the page's current `member` links in the same transaction; the people added by this save get `alertMembersAfterCommit` with source `doc`, landing in the page's workspace, title "<first name> tagged you in a doc" (no doc content), a link to the page, push tag `doc:<page id>` and `push_log` key `doc-tag:<page id>:<London date>` (so once per page, person and day). Live saves every few seconds find the link already there and alert nobody. Agent tokens tag as the agent (its name in the alert); the assistant and personal MCP tokens tag as the person. Making a note private (`shareIn`, `moveIn`, `moveFolder`, `shareFolder`) calls `untagPrivatePages`, which commits each of the person's private or daily pages that still has `member` links with those tokens as plain text. Teammates' notes in a folder that goes private can't be seen by the person making the change, so `moveFolder` and `shareFolder` read the folder's subtree before the change and, after commit, `untagFolderPages` asks `private.private_tagged_pages(folder ids)` (security definer, `crm_app` only) for pages in that subtree that are no longer shared and still have `member` links, then commits each as its owner through `memberActor`, one transaction per page, with the same version entry and live-copy update. These cleanups always save a version but leave `edited_at` and the last editor alone, so the note doesn't jump up recency lists. A note whose owner has left the workspace is skipped and logged: nobody can see it, and `takeOverFormerMemberDocs` runs `untagPrivatePages` for the person taking it over, which turns its tags into text then. Sharing never alerts on its own: a private page holds no member tokens, so "Share and tag" in the editor shares first and inserts the token as soon as the share call succeeds (before the page and nav refresh), and that save alerts. The editor takes focus inside the dialog's click handlers so the iPhone keyboard comes back. Without a live connection, the editor adopts the saved body when the server removed member tags from it (and no save or hold is pending), applied as a minimal change so the caret stays put, so a stripped token doesn't stay as a chip. The dialog prevents Radix's close autofocus so focus stays in the editor. Member tokens written into private notes before this change (possible only for a few hours through the assistant or MCP) are not cleaned retroactively; they are stripped on the next save.

**Versions.** A person's save takes a snapshot when the last one is more than 10 minutes old, and always on rename. An assistant or agent edit (and a restore, a link rewrite, or a save with `overwrite` after a conflict) first snapshots the current text if it differs from the latest version, then snapshots the result. Restoring creates a new version. Retention keeps the newest 100 versions per page and, beyond those, the last version of each London day for a year.

**Audit.** Changes to pages and folders that are shared (or were until that change) write `page.*` and `page_folder.*` audit events, which feed webhooks. Private pages, daily notes included, are not audited. `page.updated` is written when a version is taken, not on every keystroke save. Nothing is deleted: archiving a folder archives everything in it with the same timestamp, and restoring it brings back exactly those.

**Search.** `searchDocs` ranks exact, prefix and substring title matches, trigram similarity (`similarity` and `word_similarity`, through whichever schema `pg_trgm` lives in) and a prefix `tsquery` over the search column, with a `ts_headline` snippet for body matches.

**Daily notes.** One `daily` page per person per London date (`appTimeZone()`), titled with the ISO date, created on first open or append. `appendToDailyNote` and the jot line add `HH:MM text` entries separated by a blank line (`timedEntry` in `lib/docs/markdown.ts` puts the time on its own line when the entry starts with a heading, list, quote, code fence, table or rule, so that markdown still renders, and the editor shows an older `HH:MM ## heading` line as a heading) (`lib/docs/daily.ts` parses them), optionally with a line of the day's meetings from `listCalendarItems`.

**Leaving.** `private.team_remove` is unchanged: a removed member's docs stay owned by them and, if private, visible to nobody (their shared docs stay shared). `private.docs_reassign` lets an owner or admin take over a former member's notes and folders (`takeOverFormerMemberDocs`); daily notes are never reassigned.

**Agents.** The assistant reads docs through `search_knowledge` and `read_knowledge` and changes them with `save_doc` (see `docs/assistant.md`, Docs, for the "assistant can edit without asking" rule). MCP tools are in `server/mcp/docs.ts` and REST routes under `/api/v1/docs`.

**The Docs area.** `app/(crm)/docs/[[...slug]]` renders `components/docs/docs-screen.tsx`: a list pane, the note in a CodeMirror 6 editor (`lib/docs/editor/`, a Lezer grammar for `[[links]]`, `#tags` and `@` mentions that uses the same tag rules as `lib/docs/markdown.ts`), and a right panel for backlinks or version history. The sidebar Docs section, the palette, the shortcuts and the record pages' Notes sections share one Zustand store (`stores/docs-store.ts`), which talks to the `DocsData` interface in `lib/docs/client-types.ts`. The real adapter (`lib/docs/live-adapter.ts`) reads through route handlers under `/api/docs` so reads never queue behind server actions, saves through `PUT /api/docs/pages/<id>`, and makes other changes with the server actions in `app/(crm)/docs/actions.ts`. `lib/docs/http.ts` holds the handlers, which run as the signed-in person through `withActor`, return `{ error, code, details }` with the `CrmError` status, and are tested directly in `tests/docs-routes.test.ts`. `lib/docs/view.ts` is the one place `lib/docs` types become the UI's types. `lib/docs/server.ts` loads the first paint. The `(crm)` layout doesn't wait for Docs: it passes an empty nav, and the provider fetches `/api/docs/nav` straight away on `/docs` and when the browser is idle elsewhere, so the sidebar's folders and tags fill in a moment later. The store starts (titles, activity) only on `/docs`.

| Route | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/docs/nav` | Folders, the tag tree with page counts per prefix, the daily note count and today's note. |
| `GET /api/docs/pages?list=all\|folder\|tag\|daily\|search` | Summaries for a list (a folder includes its subfolders), with `sort`. |
| `GET` and `PUT /api/docs/pages/<id>` | A note with its Markdown, or an autosave with `title`, `body`, `baseRevision` and `overwrite`. A stale `baseRevision` answers 409 with `code: "conflict"` and the latest note; the editor keeps the person's text and offers Keep mine (a save with `overwrite`, which versions both texts) or Use theirs. |
| `GET /api/docs/pages/<id>/versions`, `.../versions/<versionId>`, `.../backlinks` | History (bodies load one version at a time), backlinks and unlinked mentions. |
| `GET /api/docs/recent`, `/search`, `/link-targets`, `/titles` | ⌘K, the `[[` picker (notes, matching headings and a daily note) and the map from lowercased title (or a daily note's date) to id used to draw links. |
| `GET /api/docs/mentioning?kind=&id=`, `/records?refs=` | Notes that mention a record, and the names and details of records mentioned in a note. |
| `GET /api/docs/daily`, `POST /api/docs/daily/<date>`, `GET /api/docs/meetings?date=` | Days with entries, opening (or creating) a daily note, and that day's meetings. |
| `GET /api/docs/activity` | Notes the assistant edited in the last minute, with their revisions. The adapter polls it every 20 seconds on Docs pages (every 2 minutes once the live activity channels are joined) and after an approved `save_doc`, shows "Orbit assistant is writing" for those notes, and reloads a cached note that isn't open live and has no unsaved changes. |
| `GET` and `POST /api/docs/pages/<id>/live` | The live copy. `GET` returns the stored Yjs state (`state`, base64), creating it from the Markdown on first open, plus its `generation`, the revision and the transport (`supabase`, `relay` or null). `POST` takes `update` (the client's changes since the server state it knows), `vector` (its state vector), `generation` and `title`, merges them into the stored state, and answers with the server's `vector`, the `missing` changes the client lacks, `generation`, `rebuilt`, the revision and the note's summary. With `rebuilt: true` the update wasn't applied and `missing` is the whole new state (see Limits and compaction). Errors: `live_too_large` (413, the request or update is over the limit, save the classic way instead), `too_long` (413, the merged text is over 1,000,000 characters, don't retry), `live_invalid` (400, the update holds something other than plain text). `GET`, and a `POST` without changes, record the person as watching the note; a `POST` with changes refreshes that only when it is over 30 seconds old. |
| `GET` and `POST /api/docs/live/relay` | The local relay (only with `DOCS_LIVE_RELAY=1` and no Supabase): an event stream per topic and a send, checked against the same visibility as Realtime. |

Moves call `moveDoc` and `moveFolder` without `allowVisibilityChange` first; a `visibility_change` answer opens a confirmation that says who gains or loses access, and confirming retries with it.

### Live editing

Each open note is a Yjs document (`lib/docs/live/`) with one `Y.Text` named `body` that the editor binds to through `y-codemirror.next`'s sync plugin. Markdown in `content` stays the source of truth for search, links, tags, versions and agents; `ydoc` is kept equal to it.

**Opening.** The note paints from Markdown as before. After the first frame the editor loads `lib/docs/live/client.ts`, waits for any classic save in flight, fetches the live copy and joins the private channel `doc:<id>`. While it attaches, classic saves for that note are held (the text is only marked unsaved, and written to the device if the tab hides); it then settles any classic save, fetches the live copy and settles again, refetching if a save landed in between. Text typed before the copy arrived is rebased onto it (`lib/docs/live/rebase.ts`, a three-way merge from the last text the server confirmed), so nothing is lost or doubled. If attaching fails, the held edit goes through the classic save. A note switched away from keeps its Yjs document with its cached editor state, so switching back reattaches without reloading. Without a live transport (local development without Supabase or the relay) the editor stays on the classic save path.

**Sync protocol** (`LiveSession` in `lib/docs/live/session.ts`, JSON broadcasts carrying base64 Yjs bytes). On joining, a client sends `hello` with its state vector, its pending changes as `update`, and its awareness; the current saver answers `hello` with `state` (what the newcomer lacks) and everyone answers with their awareness. Local changes are batched for 80 ms and sent as `update` with the sender's client id and sequence number, with awareness (cursor, selection, name) bundled when it changed; awareness alone is throttled to 120 ms. A change too big for one broadcast (over 180,000 characters) is saved first and announced with `pull`. Messages that arrive out of order wait inside Yjs until what they depend on arrives, and a client only ever sends the server changes it has fully received. `hello`, `state`, `update` and `saved` carry the live copy's generation `g`; Yjs bytes from another generation are ignored and the client syncs instead. Peer messages are untrusted: a malformed one is dropped, a `server` flag from a peer is ignored, and `saved`, `pull` and `title` only prompt a sync with the server. Assistant and agent cursors are labelled as such only while a `writing` event for that note and agent (which only the server can send) is active; otherwise they show as a person.

**Saving.** One client saves for everyone: the lowest awareness client id among the people in the note. It posts its changes since the last server state 1.2 seconds after the last change (at most 4 seconds after the first), then broadcasts `saved` with the server's state vector and revision. A `saved` message is only a hint: a client with unconfirmed changes of its own, or one behind the vector, syncs with the server, and a change counts as confirmed only when that client's own sync carried it and the server's reply covers it. Anyone whose own changes stay unconfirmed for 6 seconds saves them itself. A departing saver saves and announces `saved` before it leaves the channel. Every client syncs with the server every 30 seconds while visible (without a channel, every 8 seconds while it has unsaved changes, backing off to a minute otherwise) and when the tab returns, comes back online or reconnects. The status reads Live with a joined channel and Offline only when the browser is offline or the server can't be reached; without a channel it shows the usual Saving and Saved. A 403 or 404 stops the session, leaves the channel, makes the note read-only and says it doesn't exist or is private to someone else; 401 asks to sign in again; other 400, 409 and `too_long` errors stop retrying and show the error until the next edit; `live_too_large` and `live_invalid` hand the note to the classic save. Network errors, 408, 429 and 5xx retry with backoff. When a reply says the copy was `rebuilt`, the client builds a new Yjs document from `missing`, rebases its text onto it from the last confirmed server text, rebinds the editor and syncs again, resending any pending title. On `pagehide` a client removes its cursor first, then sends its unconfirmed changes with a keepalive request when they fit in 60,000 characters, and keeps them on the device either way. The route merges with `Y.applyUpdate` under the row lock (`syncLiveDoc` in `lib/docs/store.ts`), so concurrent or repeated saves are harmless, then derives the Markdown and saves it through `commitPage` (versions every 10 minutes per person, links, tags, search). A merge that changes nothing textual stores the Yjs state without a new revision. Unconfirmed changes are also kept in local storage as a Yjs update tagged with the generation (up to 750,000 bytes, falling back to only this client's changes; when neither fits or storage is full the note warns that the text isn't kept on the device) and merged back on the next open. Titles travel with the next save and are announced with `title`.

**Server-side changes.** `commitPage` keeps `ydoc` in step for every other write (classic saves, REST, MCP, the assistant, restores, link rewrites): it diffs the stored text against the new Markdown (`lib/docs/live/text-diff.ts`, Myers on lines then characters, never splitting a surrogate pair) and applies the result as `Y.Text` deletes and inserts, so typing elsewhere survives. After the transaction commits (`afterCommit`, run through `after()`), `publishLiveChange` in `lib/docs/live/broadcast.ts` posts the update to Realtime's REST broadcast endpoint with the service key (`POST /realtime/v1/api/broadcast/<topic>/events/<event>?private=true`, falling back to the batch `POST /realtime/v1/api/broadcast` with `private: true` on each message when an older Realtime answers 404, each request with a 5 second timeout), only if someone opened the note in the last 90 seconds (`crm.page_watchers`, read through `private.page_watched`). Without `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` nothing is published, and open notes pick the change up on their next sync, within 30 seconds; the seed's `livePublish` says which.
